\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\inf_{s\in[\varepsilon^{\prime},1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)]}\left({n^{-1}\bm{V}(t_{s-\varepsilon^{\prime}})-1+s-\varepsilon^{\prime}}\right)\leq-\varepsilon^{\prime}/2}\right)\leq\frac{2a_{n}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}.

Recall from
eq.

5.5
and
eq.

5.6
that

n^{-1}\bm{V}(\bm{\tau}_{n,s})=1-n^{-1}\lfloor ns\rfloor
or
1-n^{-1}(\lfloor ns\rfloor+1)
almost surely. We then conclude from
Equation

5.8
and
Equation

5.9
that, for
n
large enough,

(5.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\exists s\in[\varepsilon^{\prime},1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon^{\prime}]:n^{-1}\bm{V}(\bm{\tau}_{n,s})\notin\left({n^{-1}\bm{V}(t_{s+\varepsilon^{\prime}})+\varepsilon^{\prime}/4,n^{-1}\bm{V}(t_{s-\varepsilon^{\prime}})-\varepsilon^{\prime}/4}\right)}\right)\leq\frac{4a_{n}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}.

Since
\bm{V}(\cdot)
is a nonincreasing function,
eq.

5.10
immediately gives that

(5.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\exists s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]:\bm{\tau}_{n,s}\notin\left[{t_{s-\varepsilon},t_{s+\varepsilon}}\right]}\right)=o_{n}(1).

Proof of (ii):
By
eq.

5.5
and
eq.

5.6
,
\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=\bm{V}_{k}(\bm{\tau}_{n,s})
or
\bm{V}_{k}(\bm{\tau}_{n,s})+1
almost surely. The combination of
Lemma

5.5
,
eq.

5.11
and the
dominated convergence theorem gives that for any
\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,\varepsilon)
and
\tilde{a}_{n}:=3\max\{4a_{n},1/n\}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\sup_{s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]}\left|{\frac{\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}{n}-\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s}}p_{k}}\right|}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\sup_{s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]}\left|{\frac{\bm{V}_{k}(t_{s-\varepsilon^{\prime}})}{n}-\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s}}p_{k}}\right|}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\sup_{s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]}\left|{\frac{\bm{V}_{k}(t_{s+\varepsilon^{\prime}})}{n}-\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s}}p_{k}}\right|}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{P}\left({\exists\ s\in[\varepsilon^{\prime},1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon^{\prime}]:\bm{\tau}_{n,s}\notin\left[{t_{s-\varepsilon^{\prime}},t_{s+\varepsilon^{\prime}}}\right]}\right)+o_{n}(1)

(5.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left({\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s-\varepsilon^{\prime}}}p_{k}-\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s}}p_{k}}\right)+\left({\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s}}p_{k}-\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s+\varepsilon^{\prime}}}p_{k}}\right)+\frac{\tilde{a}_{n}}{\varepsilon^{\prime}}+o_{n}(1).

On the other hand, since
t_{s}
is the unique zero of
H_{s}(t)=\sigma(t)-1+s
in
[0,1-p_{0})
, by the inverse function theorem,
t_{s}
is a continuously differentiable function with respect to
s
. Differentiating both sides of
H_{s}(t_{s})=0
with respect to
s
gives that

(5.13)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t_{s}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s}=\left({\sum_{k=0}^{K}p_{k}k\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s}}}\right)^{-1}=\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}\in[K^{-1}(1-s-p_{0})^{-1},(1-s-p_{0})^{-1}],

where we use the fact that
K^{-1}\lambda(t)\leq\sigma(t)-p_{0}\leq\lambda(t)
(recall (
5.2
)).
The combination of
eq.

5.13
and the mean value theorem then gives that

(5.14)

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s-\varepsilon^{\prime}}}p_{k}-\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s}}p_{k}\leq k\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s-\varepsilon^{\prime}}}p_{k}(t_{s}-t_{s-\varepsilon^{\prime}})\leq kp_{k}\varepsilon^{\prime}\left({\sigma(-\ln\xi)-p_{0}}\right)^{-1},

and

(5.15)

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s}}p_{k}-\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s+\varepsilon^{\prime}}}p_{k}\leq k\mathrm{e}^{-kt_{s}}p_{k}(t_{s+\varepsilon^{\prime}}-t_{s})\leq kp_{k}\varepsilon^{\prime}\left({\sigma(-\ln\xi)-p_{0}}\right)^{-1}.

The desired result now follows from the combination of
section

5.2
to
eq.

5.15
and taking
\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon^{\prime}(n)=\min\{\varepsilon,\tilde{a}_{n}^{1/2}\}
in
section

5.2
.

Proof of (iii):
Given
Lemma

5.4
, the proof is analogous to the proof of item (ii).

Proof of (iv):

By
eq.

5.5
and
eq.

5.6
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mbox{\rm\ref{exploration_step1} is performed in the process of awakening vertices $\varepsilon n,\ldots,(1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon)n$}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left({\mbox{\rm\ref{exploration_step1} is not performed between $\bm{\tau}_{n,\varepsilon-1/n}$ and $\bm{\tau}_{n,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon+1/n}$}}\right).

Then the desired result is a direct consequence of the combination of
eq.

5.4
and
eq.

5.11
.
∎

5.3
Current degrees in the removal model

In this section,
we prove the following lemma, which provides a good estimate of the number of vertices with current degree
k
at the time when
\lfloor sn\rfloor
vertices have been awakened in the graph exploration:

Lemma 5.9
.

For each integer
0\leq k\leq K
and
\varepsilon>0
, uniformly in
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\frac{1}{n}\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-\sum_{m=k}^{K}\binom{m}{k}\left({\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{k}\left({1-\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{m-k}\mathrm{e}^{-mt_{s}}p_{m}}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

The proof of
Lemma

5.9
requires the following lemma on estimating the ratio of two random variables:

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
\left({\bm{X}_{n}}\right)_{n\geq 1}
,
\left({\bm{Y}_{n}}\right)_{n\geq 1}
and
\left({\bm{Z}_{n}}\right)_{n\geq 1}
be three sequences of random variables defined on the same probability space such that
0\leq\bm{X}_{n}\leq C\bm{Y}_{n}
for some constant
C>0
,
\bm{Y_{n}}>0
for all
n
and
\left({\bm{Z}_{n}}\right)_{n\geq 1}\subseteq[0,1]
. Let

\left({\mathfrak{H}_{n}}\right)_{n\geq 0}
be a sequence of events
with
\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{H}_{n}}\right)\geq 1-a_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
and a sequence
(a_{n})_{n\geq 1}\subseteq(0,1]
. Finally, assume that there exist
x\geq 0,y>0
and
\beta>0
such that

(5.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}_{\mathfrak{H}_{n}}\left|{\bm{X}_{n}-xn^{\beta}}\right|}\right]\leq a_{n}n^{\beta}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}_{\mathfrak{H}_{n}}\left|{\bm{Y}_{n}-yn^{\beta}}\right|}\right]\leq a_{n}n^{\beta}.

Then for
n
such that
a_{n}\leq y^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\frac{\bm{X}_{n}}{\bm{Y}_{n}}\bm{Z}_{n}-\frac{x}{y}\bm{Z}_{n}}\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\left|{\frac{\bm{X}_{n}}{\bm{Y}_{n}}-\frac{x}{y}}\right|\leq\frac{(x+y)\sqrt{a_{n}}}{y(y-\sqrt{a_{n}})}+\left({C+\frac{x}{y}}\right)(2\sqrt{a_{n}}+a_{n}).

Lemma

5.10
can be seen as a generalization of the comparison of drawing with and without replacement: For example, fixing positive integers
u,v,w,k_{1}
and
k_{2}
,
Lemma

5.10
with
\bm{X}_{n}=\binom{un}{k_{1}}\binom{vn}{k_{2}}
,
\bm{Y}_{n}=\binom{wn}{k_{1}+k_{2}}
,
\bm{Z}_{n}=1
gives that

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{\binom{un}{k_{1}}\binom{vn}{k_{2}}}{\binom{wn}{k_{1}+k_{2}}}-\binom{k_{1}+k_{2}}{k_{1}}\frac{u^{k_{1}}v^{k_{2}}}{w^{k_{1}+k_{2}}}}\right|=o_{n}(1).

The proof of
Lemma

5.10
is deferred to Appendix
C
.

Proof of
Lemma

5.9
.

Let
\mathfrak{L}_{i}
denote the event that there is no self-loop attached to vertex
i
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}
for
j\in[n]
. Similarly, for distinct
i,j\in[n]
, let
\mathfrak{L}_{i,j}
denote the event that there are no edges between vertices
i
and
j
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}
. Thanks to
4.1
,

(5.17)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{L}_{i}^{\mathrm{c}}}\right)\leq\frac{d_{i}(d_{i}-1)}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}d_{k}-1}=\bar{o}_{n}\left({1}\right),\qquad\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{L}_{i,j}^{\mathrm{c}}}\right)\leq\frac{d_{i}d_{j}}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}d_{k}-1}=\bar{o}_{n}(1),

i.e.,
\mathfrak{L}_{j}
and
\mathfrak{L}_{i,j}
are w.h.p. events. Note that both estimates are uniform over the choice of
i,j\in[n]
.
We next use the second-moment method to prove that
\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
concentrates around its average.

Analysis of
\mathbb{E}\left[{\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right]
:

Recall that
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
denotes the set of sleeping vertices after
\lfloor sn\rfloor
vertices have been awakened and
\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
from
5.6
(iii).
By the tower property and
Equation

5.17
, uniformly in
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}\mathfrak{L}_{i}\cdot\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}=k}\right\}}\big|\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},~\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},~\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right]}\right]+\bar{o}_{n}(n).

Conditionally on
\mathfrak{L}_{i}
and the values of
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
and
\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
, for
i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
, the neighbors of its half-edges are chosen uniformly from
\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}
sleeping half-edges and
\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
active half-edges without replacement. As a consequence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}=k}\right\}}|\mathfrak{L}_{i},\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\binom{\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}}{k}\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}{d_{i}-k}}{\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}}{d_{i}}}\mid\mathfrak{L}_{i},\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right].

Therefore, another application of the tower property yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right]=

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right\}}\frac{\binom{\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}}{k}\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}{d_{i}-k}}{\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}}{d_{i}}}}\right]+\bar{o}_{n}(n).

By
Lemma

5.8
and
Lemma

5.10
in Appendix
C
on the comparison between sampling with and without replacement,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right\}}\frac{\binom{\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}}{k}\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}{d_{i}-k}}{\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}}{d_{i}}}-\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right\}}\binom{d_{i}}{k}\frac{\lambda(t_{s})^{k}(\lambda(0)\mathrm{e}^{-2t_{s}}-\lambda(t_{s}))^{d_{i}-k}}{(\lambda(0)\mathrm{e}^{-2t_{s}})^{d_{i}}}}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

On the other hand, the number of vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
with original degree
m
is
\bm{V}_{m,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
, whose expectation has been estimated in
Lemma

5.8
. Hence, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right\}}\binom{d_{i}}{k}\frac{\lambda(t_{s})^{k}(\lambda(0)\mathrm{e}^{-2t_{s}}-\lambda(t_{s}))^{d_{i}-k}}{(\lambda(0)\mathrm{e}^{-2t_{s}})^{d_{i}}}}\right]+\bar{o}_{n}(n)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m=k}^{K}\binom{m}{k}\left({\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{k}\left({1-\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{m-k}\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{V}_{m,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right]+\bar{o}_{n}(n)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\sum_{m=k}^{K}\binom{m}{k}\left({\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{k}\left({1-\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{m-k}\mathrm{e}^{-mt_{s}}p_{m}+\bar{o}_{n}(n).

Analysis of
\mathbb{E}\left[{\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}^{2}}\right]
:
By
eq.

5.17
, we can compute the second moment of
\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}^{2}}\right]=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({i,j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}=\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s}=k}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[n],i\neq j}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}{\left\{{i,j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\mathfrak{L}_{i},\mathfrak{L}_{j},\mathfrak{L}_{i,j}}\right\}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}=\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s}=k}\right\}}|\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right]}\right]+\bar{o}_{n}(n^{2}).

As in the first moment computation, using
Lemma

5.8
and
Lemma

5.10
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[n],i\neq j}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{\mathds{1}{\left\{{i,j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right\}}\binom{\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}-d_{j}}{k}\binom{\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}-d_{j}-k}{k}\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}{d_{i}-k}\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}+k}{d_{j}-k}}{\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-d_{i}-d_{j}}{d_{i}}\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-2d_{i}-d_{j}}{d_{j}}}\mid\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}\Big]}\right]+\bar{o}_{n}(n^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{2}\left({\sum_{m=k}^{K}\binom{m}{k}\left({\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{k}\left({1-\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{m-k}\mathrm{e}^{-mt_{s}}p_{m}}\right)^{2}+\bar{o}_{n}(n^{2}).

As

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-n\sum_{m=k}^{K}\binom{m}{k}\left({\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{k}\left({1-\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{m-k}\mathrm{e}^{-mt_{s}}p_{m}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[{\left|{\bar{\bm{V}}_{k,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}-n\sum_{m=k}^{K}\binom{m}{k}\left({\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{k}\left({1-\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{m-k}\mathrm{e}^{-mt_{s}}p_{m}}\right|^{2}}\right]}=\bar{o}_{n}(n),

the desired result follows from the second-moment method.
∎

In light of
Lemma

5.9
, we define

\displaystyle\psi_{t}(\alpha):

\displaystyle=\sigma(t)^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{K}\alpha^{k}\sum_{m=k}^{K}\binom{m}{k}\left({\frac{\lambda(t)\mathrm{e}^{2t}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{k}\left({1-\frac{\lambda(t)\mathrm{e}^{2t}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{m-k}\mathrm{e}^{-mt}p_{m}

\displaystyle=\sigma(t)^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{K}p_{k}\mathrm{e}^{-kt}\left({1+\frac{\lambda(t)\mathrm{e}^{2t}}{\lambda(0)}(\alpha-1)}\right)^{k}.

By
Lemma

5.9
, we can regard
\psi_{t_{s}}
as the limiting generating function of the current degree distribution of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
. Finally, we set

(5.18)

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{t}(\alpha):=\lambda(t)^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{K}kp_{k}\mathrm{e}^{-kt}\left({1+\frac{\lambda(t)\mathrm{e}^{2t}}{\lambda(0)}(\alpha-1)}\right)^{k-1}=\psi_{t}^{\prime}(\alpha)/\psi_{t}^{\prime}(1).

5.4
The choice of the next awakened vertex

In this section, we take a closer look at vertex
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}

and its probabilistic properties. Recall that the current degree of vertex
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
is denoted by
\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s}
. The current degree
\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s}
of vertex
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
deviates from its original degree
d_{j}
if and only if
j
is adjacent to one of the previously awakened vertices
\{\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}\}
. According to the graph exploration, the first half-edge of a vertex in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
to be killed is either a half-edge connected to a vertex outside
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
when
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})>0
, or an arbitrary half-edge in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
when
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})=0
. This leads to the following definition, which characterizes all possible choices of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
:

Definition 5.11

(Hypnopompic half-edge)
.

Given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
,
a half-edge
h
incident to vertex
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
is called hypnopompic if one of the following holds:

•

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})=0
;

•

d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s}>0
and
h
is not one of the
\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s}
half-edges that connect
j
to a vertex in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
.

∎

The following lemma shows that given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
and thus a set of hypnopompic half-edges incident to vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
, each of the latter is equally likely to be the half-edge that is killed in the step of the graph exploration that awakens
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
:

Lemma 5.12
.

Fix
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
. Given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G
,
let
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
be two hypnopompic half-edges, and
\bm{h}
be the first half-edge incident to a vertex in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
that is declared dead. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{h}=h_{1}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{h}=h_{2}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G}\right).

Moreover, for any half-edge
h
incident to a vertex
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
that is not hypnopompic,
\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{h}=h\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G}\right)=0
.

Proof.

Observe that the set of non-hypnopompic half-edges
h
is only non-empty if
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
is such that
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s})>0
. In this case, conditioning on
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G
entails that in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}
, by definition, the subgraph
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
is connected to the vertices in
\{\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}\}
via a hypnopompic half-edge. Therefore, in the graph exploration, such an edge will be the first to be explored and thus killed. This implies the second claim.

For the remainder of the proof, fix
G
and two hypnopompic half-edges
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
. Let
\textbf{Hist}_{s}
denote the (random) history of the vertex-removal graph exploration up to, but excluding the step when
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is awakened. Thus, each possible realization of
\textbf{Hist}_{s}
records the sequence of steps that have been carried out, together with the half-edges that are activated or killed and the vertices that are awakened in each step. Its last component either consists of the step in which
\bm{\nu}_{s}
is awakened and its adjacent half-edges are activated or a step in which two half-edges between vertices in
\{\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}\}
are paired and thus killed, but where no new vertex is awakened.

By Bayes’ Theorem, for
i\in\{1,2\}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{h}=h_{i}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{H}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left({{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G\mid\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H,~\bm{h}=h_{i}}\right)\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{h}=h_{i}\mid\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H}\right)\mathbb{P}\left({\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H}\right)}{\mathbb{P}\left({{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G}\right)}.

Thus, to show the claim, it is sufficient to show that for all histories
H
that are compatible with
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G
,

(5.19)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{h}=h_{1}\mid\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{h}=h_{2}\mid\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H}\right)\quad\text{and}

(5.20)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G\mid\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H,~\bm{h}=h_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left({{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G\mid\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H,~\bm{h}=h_{2}}\right).

Given a history
H
, it is already determined whether
\bm{h}
, the next half-edge to be killed, will be killed by
Step 2
(if all half-edges adjacent to
\{\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}\}
have been explored) or
Step 3
(otherwise). If
H
is such that
\bm{h}
is killed by
Step 2
, then conditionally on
H
, the next step of the graph exploration is to awaken
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
through
Step 1
by choosing a sleeping half-edge
uniformly
at random. Thus, as both
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
are sleeping, they are chosen with the same conditional probability. If
H
is such that
\bm{h}
is killed by
Step 3
, then conditionally on
H
, the next step of the graph exploration is to kill a half-edge in
Step 3
based on its life-time. Conditionally on
H
, we know that the half-edge with the lowest life-time is adjacent to a vertex in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
, but not more than that. Again, by exchangeability of the lifetimes,
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
are chosen with the same conditional probability. This establishes (
5.19
).

Next, also in the consideration of (
5.20
), we distinguish two cases. First, assume that
H
is such that
\bm{h}
is chosen according to
Step 2
, which means that in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}
, the subgraph
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
is disconnected from the vertices in
\{\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}\}
, and that
\bm{h}
is part of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
. Conditioning on
\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H,~\bm{h}=h_{i}
therefore potentially starts the exploration of the components of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
from different half-edges, but at each future step, the probability to choose the ‘right’ half-edge to pair with the last half-edge killed by
Step 2
to complete
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G
is uniformly distributed over the remaining half-edges.

Second, assume that
H
is such that
\bm{h}
is chosen according to
Step 3
, which means that in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}
, the subgraph
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
is connected to the vertices in
\{\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}\}
, and that
\bm{h}
is not part of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
. In this case, after conditioning on
\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H,~\bm{h}=h_{i}
, not only unpaired half-edges in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
are remaining, but also unpaired half-edges adjacent to vertices in
\{\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}\}
. However, again, at each future step, the probability to choose a particular second half-edge to pair with the last half-edge killed by
Step 2
is uniformly distributed over the remaining half-edges, and the number of possible pairings between hypnopomic half-edges and unpaired half-edges in
\{\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}\}
is the same given
\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H,~\bm{h}=h_{1}
and given
\textbf{Hist}_{s}=H,~\bm{h}=h_{2}
. This establishes (
5.20
).
∎

In particular,
Lemma

5.12
implies that given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
, the probability that any sleeping vertex in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
is chosen as the next vertex to be awakened is either proportional to its original degree or to the number of half-edges that connect it to the set of awake vertices:

Corollary 5.13
.

Given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
, the following two cases can occur:

(i)

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})=0
: In this case,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is chosen among the vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}

with probability proportional to the original degree sequence
(d_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}
.

(ii)

\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})\neq 0
: In this case,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is chosen among the vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
with probability proportional to
(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}
, i.e., the number of half-edges that connect it to the vertices
\{\bm{\nu}_{1},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}\}
.

6
Graph decomposition and rank

6.1
The type of the next awakened vertex

Given the previous results about the choice of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
, we next return to the associated adjacency matrices. Denote by
\bm{A}_{n,s}
the matrix in which the rows and columns corresponding to
\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\bm{\nu}_{2/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}
in
\bm{A}_{n}
are replaced by zero rows and columns, respectively. Then excluding those zero rows and columns,
\bm{A}_{n,s}
can be viewed as the adjacency matrix of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
. By a slight abuse of terminology, from now on, we refer to
\bm{A}_{n,s}
as the adjacency matrix of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
.

According to the proof strategy in
Section

3.4
, our primary goal is to compute the probability that the newly awakened vertex
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is of a specific type, conditional on

\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
, the perturbed adjacency matrix of the induced subgraph of sleeping vertices before its awakening. Recall that the matrix
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
is fully determined by
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
and
\mathbf{\Theta}
.
In this context,
Corollary

5.13
inspires the following definitions, which correspond to the conditional probability that
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}

has a certain type in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
:

Definition 6.1

(Size-biased type proportions)
.

Given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
and
\mathbf{\Theta}=(\mathbf{\Theta}_{r}[\theta_{r},n],\mathbf{\Theta}_{c}[n,\theta_{c}])
, define

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}_{s}:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})},&\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})\neq 0;\\
\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}d_{i}}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}d_{i}},&\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})=0;\end{cases}

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{s}:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})},&\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})\neq 0;\\
\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}d_{i}}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}d_{i}},&\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})=0,\end{cases}

for
\bm{w}\in\left\{{\bm{x},\bm{y},\bm{z},\bm{u},\bm{v}}\right\}
. We also abbreviate
\bm{\zeta}_{s}=(\bm{x}_{s},\bm{y}_{s},\bm{z}_{s},\bm{u}_{s},\bm{v}_{s})
and call the components of this vector the size-biased type proportions.
∎

In terms of the size-biased type proportions,
Corollary

5.13
reads as follows:

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
\bm{w}\in\left\{{\bm{x},\bm{y},\bm{z},\bm{u},\bm{v}}\right\}
and
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s}}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)=\bm{w}_{s}.

Proof.

Given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
, its vertex set
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
and induced degrees
(\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}
are fixed.

Case 1:

If
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})=0
, by
Corollary

5.13

(i)
, for each sleeping vertex
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
,

(6.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}=j\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right)=\frac{d_{j}}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}d_{i}}.

Since the perturbation matrices
\mathbf{\Theta}
are independent of the exploration and the configuration model
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}
,
eq.

6.1
gives that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cap\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}=j\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)=\bm{w}_{s}.

Case 2:

If
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})\neq 0
, by
Corollary

5.13

(ii)
, for each sleeping vertex
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
,

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}=j\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right)=\frac{d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s}}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}.

As in
Case
\bm{1}
, it follows that
\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)=\bm{w}_{s}
.

Finally, the missing identity follows from the tower property:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s}}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s}}\right]=\bm{w}_{s}.

∎

6.2
Rank and types

We now relate the graph decomposition procedure and the size-biased proportions of types to our main objective, the derivation of a lower bound on the expected rank for supercritical configuration models. Abbreviating
\iota:=1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon
, we obtain the lower bound

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=\lfloor\varepsilon n\rfloor}^{\lceil n\iota\rceil-1}\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,j/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)-\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,(j+1)/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,\lceil n\iota\rceil/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right].

Further, by the choice of
\iota
,
\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,\lceil n\iota\rceil/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]
effectively corresponds to a rank computation for a subcritical configuration model.
Thus, we will be mainly concerned with estimating the rank differences in
Equation

6.3
.

For this, we define the event

(6.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}=\left\{{\mathbf{\Theta}_{r}[\bm{\theta}_{r},n](,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})=0_{\bm{\theta}_{r}\times 1},\mathbf{\Theta}_{c}[n,\bm{\theta}_{c}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)=0_{1\times\bm{\theta}_{c}}}\right\}.

For
\mathbf{\Theta}
in
\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
,
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n};\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}\rrbracket=\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
.
Moreover,
3.10
ensures that

(6.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}}\right)=1+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Then by the deterministic rank relation
Equation

3.3
and
Lemma

6.2
on the type of the next awakened vertex, for
\mathbf{\Theta}\in\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)-\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid\mathbf{\Theta}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{X}(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}])\mid\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)+2\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{Y}(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}])\mid\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{U}(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}])\mid\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{V}(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}])\mid\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{x}_{s}+2\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s}\mid\mathbf{\Theta}}\right].

Combining the last identity with
Equation

6.5
gives that

(6.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)-\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{x}_{s}+2\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s}}\right]+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

(
6.6
) illustrates why it is crucial for our approach to estimate the values of the size-biased proportions of frozen types.
In the spirit of
[
26
]
, we aim
to derive fixed-point equations for the size-biased type proportions. These equations will eventually help us to lower bound the asymptotic rank through
eq.

6.6
.

Our route towards a derivation of the fixed-point equations is the following proposition:

Proposition 6.3
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
. Assume that

(6.7)

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{\zeta}_{s}-\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right\|_{1}=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

and that there exist functions
Y,Z,U,V
such that for each
W\in\{Y,Z,U,V\}
,
W=W(\zeta,\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}})
is differentiable with respect to
\zeta=(x,y,z,u,v)\in[0,1]^{5}
with uniformly bounded gradient, and that

(6.8)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-W\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}}\right)=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Then, for any
\bm{w}\in\{\bm{y},\bm{z},\bm{u},\bm{v}\}
,

(6.9)

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{s}-W(\bm{\zeta}_{s},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}})=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

If only

(6.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-W\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}}\right)\geq\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

then

(6.11)

\displaystyle\bm{w}_{s}-W(\bm{\zeta}_{s},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}})\geq\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

The proof of
Proposition

6.3
is a direct consequence of
[
26
, Proposition C.1]
. Its derivation from that proposition is given in Appendix
G.1
.

7
Stability of types under graph decomposition

To derive the fixed-point equations for the type proportions, we first show that in the beginning of the graph decomposition, the latter remain relatively stable under the removal of a single vertex and its incident edges. Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
, and recall that we aim to derive uniform bounds for
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
, which is captured in our
o_{n}
,
\bar{o}_{n}
and
\bar{o}_{n,P}
notation. The main result of the current section demonstrates that the proportions of types remain relatively stable under a single step of the graph exploration:

Proposition 7.1

(Stability of types)
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
. For any
\bm{w}\in\{\bm{x},\bm{y},\bm{z},\bm{u},\bm{v},\bm{\zeta}\}
,

\mathbb{E}\left\|{\bm{w}_{s}-\bm{w}_{s+1/n}}\right\|_{1}=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

We prove
Proposition

7.1
in the remainder of this section.

7.1
Reduction to average type changes

In this section, we reduce the proof of
Proposition

7.1
to the task of bounding the probability that a uniformly chosen vertex changes its type in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
as compared to
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
.

For
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
, define

(7.1)

\displaystyle c=c(\varepsilon):=\min_{r\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]}\frac{\lambda(t_{r})(\lambda(0)\mathrm{e}^{-2t_{r}}-\lambda(t_{r}))}{2K\lambda(0)\mathrm{e}^{-2t_{r}}},

where
t_{s}
is the unique zero of
H_{s}(t)=\sigma(t)-1+s
in
[0,1-p_{0})
from
5.7
.
Lemma

D.1
in the Appendix shows that
n\lambda(t_{r})(\lambda(0)\mathrm{e}^{-2t_{r}}-\lambda(t_{r}))\lambda(0)^{-1}\mathrm{e}^{2t_{r}}
is a good approximation of the number of half-edges joining vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{r}\rrbracket}
to vertices outside of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{r}\rrbracket}
. Therefore,
cn
functions as a uniform lower bound on the reservoir of half-edges joining the set of sleeping vertices to the set of awake vertices during the current exploration epoch.
The combination of
eq.

5.3
and
Lemma

5.2
yields that
c>0
.

In most proofs of this section, we will work on the event

(7.2)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}:=\left\{{\text{the number of $j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}$ such that $\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s}<d_{j}$ is greater than $cn$}}\right\}.

This ensures that the number of half-edges of vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
connected to vertices in
[n]\backslash\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
remains of order
n
, thereby guaranteeing that the next vertex to be awakened will be chosen according to
Step 3
.

Lemma

D.1
in Appendix
D
shows that
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}
occurs with high probability:

(7.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{G}^{c}_{\varepsilon,s}}\right)=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

7.1.1
Proof of Proposition
7.1
, Step 1.

Recall
6.1
for the type proportions
\bm{w}\in\{\bm{x},\bm{y},\bm{z},\bm{u},\bm{v}\}
. We first relate the difference of the proportions
\bm{w}_{s}
and
\bm{w}_{s+1/n}
to the type change of a uniformly chosen vertex.
Set
\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{\varepsilon,s}:=\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}\cap\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
. As
\left|{\bm{w}_{s}-\bm{w}_{s+1/n}}\right|
is bounded above by
1
, by
Equation

7.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\bm{w}_{s}-\bm{w}_{s+1/n}}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathds{1}\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{\varepsilon,s}\left|{\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}-\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n})}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n})}}\right|\Bigg].

Moreover,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})-\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}},s)\leq K,

so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\bm{w}_{s}-\bm{w}_{s+1/n}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathds{1}\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{\varepsilon,s}\Bigg|\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s})}-\frac{\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n})}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s+1/n})}\Bigg)\Bigg|\Bigg]+\bar{o}_{n}(1).

We next use that for any
m\geq 1
and sequences
(\hat{a}_{i})_{i\in[m]},(\hat{b}_{i})_{i\in[m]},(\hat{c}_{i})_{i\in[m]},(\hat{d}_{i})_{i\in[m]}
of numbers,

(7.4)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i\in[m]}(\hat{a}_{i}\hat{b}_{i}-\hat{c}_{i}\hat{d}_{i})\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{i\in[m]}(\hat{a}_{i}\hat{b}_{i}-\hat{a}_{i}\hat{d}_{i}+\hat{d}_{i}\hat{a}_{i}-\hat{d}_{i}\hat{c}_{i})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{i\in[m]}\left|{\hat{a}_{i}}\right|\sum_{i\in[m]}\left|{\hat{b}_{i}-\hat{d}_{i}}\right|+\sup_{i\in[m]}\left|{\hat{d}_{i}}\right|\sum_{i\in[m]}\left|{\hat{a}_{i}-\hat{c}_{i}}\right|.

Moreover, on
\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{\varepsilon,s}
,
\frac{d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n}}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s+1/n})}\leq K/(cn)
, so that by
eq.

7.4
, again on
\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{\varepsilon,s}
,

\displaystyle\left|{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\Bigg(\frac{\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s})}-\frac{\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n})}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s+1/n})}\Bigg)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\left|{\frac{d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s})}-\frac{d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n}}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s+1/n})}}\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{K}{cn}\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\Delta\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}.

Furthermore,
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\left|{\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n}}\right|\leq K
since for each
i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
,
\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n}
is equal to the number of half-edges
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
connected to vertex
i
. Then, on
\bar{\mathfrak{G}}_{\varepsilon,s}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\left|{\frac{d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n}}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s+1/n})}-\frac{d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s})}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n})\left|{\frac{1}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s+1/n})}-\frac{1}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s})}}\right|+\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\left|{\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n}}\right|}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}}\right)+d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}}\right|+\frac{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\left|{\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n}}\right|}{\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})}\leq\frac{3K}{cn}=\bar{o}_{n}(1),

where in the last inequality we use
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\left|{\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n}}\right|\leq K
.
Thus we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\bm{w}_{s}-\bm{w}_{s+1/n}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{K}{cn}\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\Delta\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}\Big]+\bar{o}_{n}(1)

(7.5)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{K}{cn}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\Delta\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right)+\bar{o}_{n}(1).

7.2
Average type changes

The bound
section

7.1.1
illustrates that in order to prove the stability result
Proposition

7.1
, it is sufficient to obtain a bound on the probability of type change of an average vertex. In this sense, we show that advancing the graph exploration by a single step has a negligible effect on whether a vertex is frozen or not with respect to the associated perturbed matrices
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}],\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]

on average
. To be able to deal with all types of proportions
\bm{w}\in\{\bm{x},\bm{y},\bm{z},\bm{u},\bm{v}\}
, we also investigate freezing/unfreezing with respect to a modified matrix.
The key steps in the derivation of the desired stability result are the following two lemmas:

Lemma 7.2

(One-step matrix zeroing, original matrix)
.

Fix
\delta>0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
. Then

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left(i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\Delta\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\right)\leq 4\delta(K+1)K^{2K+2}c^{-K-1}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Lemma 7.3

(One-step matrix zeroing, row-zeroed matrix)
.

Fix
\delta>0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
. Then

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left(i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)\Delta\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)\right)\leq 4\delta(K+1)K^{2K+2}c^{-K-1}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Proof of
Proposition

7.1
.

Given the bound
section

7.1.1
along with
Lemmas

7.2
and
7.3
, the only missing step in the proof of
Proposition

7.1
is relating the events
\left\{{i\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\Delta\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}
to freezing or unfreezing events in various matrices as studied in
Lemmas

7.2
and
7.3
. However, this analysis is only a minor adaption of the proof of
[
26
, Proposition 4.11]
. We defer this proof to
Appendix

G
of the appendix. ∎

The rough proof strategy for
Lemmas

7.2
and
7.3
is similar to the approach that has been used for the weighted Erdős-Rényi random graph in
[
26
, Lemmas 4.17 and 4.18]
. However, the details differ significantly, as edges in the configuration model are not independent of each other. Therefore, we have to resort to alternative approaches, such as the moment method, to establish concentration.

7.3
One-step matrix zeroing, original matrix: Proof of
Lemma

7.2

This proof is an adaptation of the proof of
[
25
, Lemma 4.17]
. First, observe that
it is sufficient to bound

(7.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\backslash\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{P}\left({i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\backslash\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right).

Our proof strategy is to relate both freezing and unfreezing of
i
to the existence of a proper relation in either
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
or
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
that involves
i
:
On the event
\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
defined in (
6.4
),

(7.7)

\displaystyle i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\backslash\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\{i,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\}\text{ is a proper relation of }\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}],

and

\displaystyle i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\backslash\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right),\quad i\notin\{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})

(7.8)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle\left\{{i}\right\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\text{ is a proper relation of }\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}].

We defer the proofs of the implications
Equation

7.7
and
Section

7.3
to Appendix
G
, as they are analogous to
[
26
, Lemma 4.17]
.
Given these deterministic statements, we estimate the two probabilities in
eq.

7.6
on the event

(7.9)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{n,s}=\left\{{\text{{both} $\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]$ and $\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}$ are $(\delta,\ell)$-free for $2\leq\ell\leq 2K$}}\right\}.

The benefit of
\mathfrak{R}_{n,s}
is that we can
almost
disregard the impact of short proper linear relations of
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
and its transpose. Moreover, by
Proposition

3.11
,

(7.10)

\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{R}_{n,s}^{c}}\right)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Given (
7.7
) - (
7.10
), we now proceed with bounding the probabilties in (
7.6
) separately.

(i)

Freezing.

We first show that, on the high-probability event
\mathfrak{R}_{n,s-1/n}
, the probability that
\{i,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\}
forms a proper relation of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
is small when averaged over
i\in[n]
.
Given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G\in\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}
, it holds true that
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}(d_{j}-\bar{d}_{j,s})\geq cn
and
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is chosen according to
Step 3
. Then, for each vertex
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
,

(7.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}=j\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G}\right)\leq\frac{d_{j}-\bar{d}_{j,s}}{\sum_{k\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\left({d_{k}-\bar{d}_{k,s}}\right)}\leq\frac{K}{cn}.

Recall from
Section

3.3
that
\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}])
is the set of all proper relations of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
.
By (
7.11
),

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{i,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}])\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G,\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\left\{{i,j}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}])}\right\}}\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}=j\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}=G}\right)

(7.12)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{K}{cn}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\left\{{i,j}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}])}\right\}}.

Recall
3.9
and
eq.

7.10
. Then
Equation

7.3
and
item

(i)
give that

(7.13)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left({\left\{{i,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}])}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}{\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{i,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}])\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)}\right]+\bar{o}_{n}(1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{K}{cn^{2}}\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{i,j}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]),\mathfrak{R}_{n,s-1/n}}\right)+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

On the other hand, on
\mathfrak{R}_{n,s-1/n}
, there are at most
\delta(n+P)^{2}
proper relation of size
2
.
Since each proper relation is counted twice in the sum
\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\left\{{i,j}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]),\mathfrak{R}_{n,s-1/n}}\right\}}
, we conclude that this sum is upper bounded by
2\delta(n+P)^{2}
. Hence,

(7.14)

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{i,j}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]),\mathfrak{R}_{n,s-1/n}}\right)\leq 2\delta(n+P)^{2}.

Combining
eq.

7.7
,
Equation

7.13
and
Equation

7.14
yields that

(7.15)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\backslash\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right)\leq 2\delta Kc^{-1}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

(ii)

Unfreezing.

Similarly to part (i), we show that the probability that
\{i\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
forms a proper relation of
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
is small when averaged over
i\in[n]
.
To this end, observe that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\{i\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{i,j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}])}\right)

(7.16)

\displaystyle\times\mathbb{P}\left({{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\},\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}\mid\left\{{i,j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}])}\right).

We next bound the two probability terms in (
7.16
) separately, by firstly showing an anticoncentration property of
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
on
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
, and secondly using that there are only few proper relations on
\mathfrak{R}_{n,s}
.

Let
\bm{k}
be the number of half-edges of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
that connect to vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
, which is an upper bound on the number of non-zero entries of row
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
in
\bm{A}_{n,s}
. We next determine the distribution of their neighbors given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
.
For this, order these
\bm{k}
half-edges arbitrarily as half-edges
1,2,\ldots,\bm{k}
.
We call a half-edge incident to vertex
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}

free after

m\in[\bm{k}]
pairings of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}

if it is neither paired with half-edges
1,\ldots m-1
of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
nor with any vertex from
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
. These are exactly the half-edges whose neighbors are unknown when conditioned on
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
and the neighbors of half-edges
1,\ldots,m-1
.

Claim 7.1
.

Given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
,
\bm{k}
and the neighbors of half-edges
1,\ldots,m-1
for
m\in[\bm{k}]
, the neighbor of half-edge
m
is chosen uniformly from all half-edges that are free after

m-1
pairings of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
.

Proof of
7.1
.

The proof uses Bayes’ theorem and is analogous to the derivation of
Lemma

5.12
.
∎

Moreover, for
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
such that
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
holds, the number of free half-edges at any stage
m
is at least
cn-K
. Let
\bm{v}_{1},\ldots,\bm{v}_{\bm{k}}\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
denote the vertices that half-edges
1,\ldots,\bm{k}
are connected to, and let
v
be a vertex incident to a half edge that is free at stage
m
. Then
by
7.1
,

(7.17)

\displaystyle\mathds{1}{\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{v}_{m}=v\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\bm{k},\bm{v}_{1},\ldots,\bm{v}_{m-1}}\right)\leq\frac{K}{cn-K}.

Multiplying (
7.17
) for all
m\in[\bm{k}]
gives that for all
v_{1},\ldots,v_{\bm{k}}\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
,

(7.18)

\displaystyle\mathds{1}{\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{v}_{1}=v_{1},\ldots,\bm{v}_{\bm{k}}=v_{\bm{k}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\bm{k}}\right)\leq\left({\frac{K}{cn-K}}\right)^{\bm{k}}.

Now, fix
k\in[K]
and
j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\in[n]
. There are at most
\bm{k}^{\bm{k}}
choices of
(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\bm{k}})
that give rise to
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})

=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}
. Summing over such
(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\bm{k}})
in (
7.18
) and using that
\bm{k}\leq K
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathds{1}{\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\mathbb{P}\left({{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\bm{k}}\right)\leq\frac{K^{2K}}{(cn-K)^{\bm{k}}}.

Since
k\leq\bm{k}\leq K
and
c<1
, we can use the tower property to remove the conditioning on
\bm{k}
:

(7.19)

\displaystyle\mathds{1}{\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\mathbb{P}\left({{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)\leq\frac{K^{2K}}{c^{K}n^{k}}(1+\bar{o}_{n}(1)).

Furthermore,
Equation

7.19
, the tower property and the fact that the support of
\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)
is independent of the perturbation matrices
\mathbf{\Theta}
give that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\},\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}\mid\left\{{i,j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}])}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{P}({\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})=\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}\},\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta})\mid\{i,\ldots,j_{k}\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}])]

(7.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{K^{2K}}{c^{K}n^{k}}+\bar{o}_{n}(n^{-k}).

Returning to (
7.16
), we have thus obtained the upper bound

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\{i\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{K^{2K}}{c^{K}n^{k+1}}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{i,j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]),\mathfrak{R}_{n,s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

As in the proof of
eq.

7.14
, the sum
\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\left\{{i,j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]),\mathfrak{R}_{n,s}}\right\}}
counts each proper relation of size
k
exactly
k
times and each proper relation of size
k+1
exactly
k+1
times. However, on
\mathfrak{R}_{n,s}
,
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
is

(\delta,\ell)
-free for
2\leq\ell\leq 2K
, so that

(7.21)

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{i,j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]),\mathfrak{R}_{n,s}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\delta(k(n+P)^{k}+(k+1)(n+P)^{k+1}).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\{i\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}])}\right)\leq\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{\delta K^{2K}(K+1)}{c^{K}}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\delta K^{2K+1}(K+1)c^{-K}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Finally, by the deterministic upper bound
\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})}\right\}}\leq K+P+1
,

(7.22)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({i\in\{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})}\right)\leq\frac{K+P+1}{n}=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Hence, by
section

7.3
,

(7.23)

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left(i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\setminus\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\right)\leq\delta K^{2K+1}(K+1)c^{-K}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Combining
eq.

7.15
and
eq.

7.23

finishes the proof of
Lemma

7.2
.∎

7.4
One-step matrix zeroing, row-zeroed matrix: Proof of
Lemma

7.3

The proof is an adaption of the proof of
[
25
, Lemma 4.18]
. Again, we relate the ‘type change’ events in question to the existence of proper relations. More precisely, for
i\in[n]
, we will show that on a reasonably likely event
\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)
,

\displaystyle i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)\Delta\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)

(7.24)

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)})\text{ is a proper relation of }\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}\text{ or of }\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}.

The proof now proceeds in four steps: First, we define the events
\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)
. Second, we derive an upper bound on the ‘average’ probability of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)^{c}
over
i\in[n]
. This requires the bulk of the work. Third, we prove the implication (
7.4
). Finally, we show that the probability of the events on the right-hand side of (
7.4
) is uniformly bounded in
i
.

Definition of
\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)
.
The event that we work on is an intersection of three events: First, set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)=\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}\cap\left\{{i\neq\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},\bm{A}_{n}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},i)=0,\mathbf{\Theta}_{r}[\bm{\theta}_{r},n](,i)=0_{\bm{\theta}_{r}\times 1},\mathbf{\Theta}_{c}[n,\bm{\theta}_{c}](i,)=0_{1\times\bm{\theta}_{c}}}\right\}.

On
\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)
,
the non-zero entries of column (or row)
i
in all involved matrices are contained in
[n]
, i.e.,

(7.25)

\displaystyle{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](,i)})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}](,i)})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}(,i)}).

Next, let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{n,2}(i)=\left\{{\text{{for} all }j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}):j\notin\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)\Delta\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)}\right\}

be the event that no element of the support of column
i
has a different type in
\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
than in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
.
Finally, let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{n,3}(i)

\displaystyle=\left\{{\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}(\delta,\ell)\text{-free for }2\leq\ell\leq K+1}\right\}\cap\left\{{\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}(\delta,\ell)\text{-free for }2\leq\ell\leq K+1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s}(i)\cap\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s+1/n}(i).

We set
\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)=\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\mathfrak{S}_{n,2}(i)\cap\mathfrak{S}_{n,3}(i)
.

Bounding the average of
\mathbb{P}(\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i))^{c}
.
We next derive an upper bound on

(7.26)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}(\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)^{c})\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}(\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)^{c})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}(\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\mathfrak{S}_{n,2}(i)^{c})+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in n}\mathbb{P}(\mathfrak{S}_{n,3}(i)^{c}).

In doing so, observe that (
6.5
) and the construction of the perturbation in
3.10
ensure that

(7.27)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)^{c}}\right)\leq\bar{o}_{n,P}(1)+\frac{K}{n}+\frac{1}{n}=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Moreover,
Proposition

3.11
implies that

(7.28)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n,3}(i)^{c}}\right)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

We are thus left with bounding the middle sum on the r.h.s. of (
7.26
). As
\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\mathfrak{S}_{n,2}(i)^{c}
involves a ‘status change’ event with respect to a matrix in which row and column
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
are replaced by a zero row and a zero column, the basic approach that we pursue to bound the last missing sum in
eq.

7.26
is similar to the one of
Lemma

7.2
. However, in the present case, the vertices for which we investigate a status change are not chosen uniformly among all vertices, but from the support of the
i
th column of
\bm{A}_{n,s}
, and we will need to address this complication.
But first, a derivation analogous to the one of implications (
7.7
) and (
7.3
) shows that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\mathfrak{S}_{n,2}(i)^{c}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\Big(\big\{\ \exists\ j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}):\left\{{j,\bm{\nu}_{n,s+1/n}}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)\big\}

\displaystyle\cup\big\{\ \exists\ j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}):\left\{{j}\right\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)\big\}

\displaystyle\cup\big\{\ \exists\ j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}):j\in\{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\big\}\Big).

Since
\bm{A}_{n}(i,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})=\bm{A}_{n}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},i)=0
as well as
\mathbf{\Theta}_{c}[n,\bm{\theta}_{c}](i,)=0_{1\times\bm{\theta}_{c}}
on
\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)
, on this event,
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
, so that we may simplify the last inclusion as stating that

(7.29)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\mathfrak{S}_{n,2}(i)^{c}

\displaystyle\subseteq\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\big(\mathfrak{E}_{n,1}(i)\cup\mathfrak{E}_{n,2}(i)\cup\mathfrak{E}_{n,3}(i)\big),

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{n,1}(i)

\displaystyle=\big\{\ \exists\ j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}):\left\{{j,\bm{\nu}_{n,s+1/n}}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)\big\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{n,2}(i)

\displaystyle=\big\{\ \exists\ j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}):\left\{{j}\right\}\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)\big\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{E}_{n,3}(i)

\displaystyle=\big\{\ \exists\ j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}):j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\big\}.

As these events involve the matrices
\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket,\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket
that arise from the removal of vertex
i
, we define a corresponding graph as follows: For
i\in[n]
, let
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}
be the graph that is induced by
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}
on the vertex set
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}\backslash\left\{{i}\right\}
. Moreover, for
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}\backslash\left\{{i}\right\}
, let
\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s,\langle{i}\rangle}
be the (current) degree of vertex
j
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}
.
We emphasize that via its vertex count, the graph
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}
contains the information whether or not
i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
.
Finally,
on the event
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}
from
eq.

7.2
,

(7.30)

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}\backslash\left\{{i}\right\}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s,\langle{i}\rangle})\geq\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}\backslash\left\{{i}\right\}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s})\geq cn-(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s})\geq cn-K.

We next bound the probability of the three events on the right-hand side of (
7.29
) separately.

1.

We first establish an upper bound on
\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{E}_{n,1}(i)}\right)
from (
7.29
).
Note that by
Proposition

3.11
and
eq.

7.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{E}_{n,1}(i)}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j,\ell\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}),\bm{\nu}_{n,s+1/n}=\ell,\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s},\left\{{j,\ell}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right),\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s}(i)}\right)

(7.31)

\displaystyle\quad+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

By the tower property and independence of the perturbation matrix and the graph structure, for each
j,\ell\in[n]
,

(7.32)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}),\bm{\nu}_{n,s+1/n}=\ell,\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s},\left\{{j,\ell}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right),\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s}(i)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}),\bm{\nu}_{n,s+1/n}=\ell,\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathds{1}{\left\{{\left\{{j,\ell}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right),\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s}(i)}\right\}}\Big].

By
eq.

7.11
, for each
j,\ell\in[n]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}),\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}=\ell,\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}{\left\{{j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}}\right\}}\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right)\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}}\right]

(7.33)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{K}{cn}\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}}\right).

As in
Lemma

5.12
and
7.1
, it can be shown that conditionally on
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}
, each vertex
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}\backslash\left\{{i}\right\}
has a probability proportional to
d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s,\langle{i}\rangle}
of being chosen as a neighbor of a half-edge of
i
.
Combined with (
7.30
), this yields

(7.34)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}}\right)\leq d_{i}\frac{d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s,\langle{i}\rangle}}{\sum_{j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}\backslash\left\{{i}\right\}}(d_{j}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s,\langle{i}\rangle})}\leq\frac{K^{2}}{cn-K}.

Plugging
eq.

7.34
into
item

1
, we arrive at

(7.35)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}),\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}=\ell,\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}}\right)\leq\frac{K^{3}}{(cn-K)^{2}}.

Plugging (
7.35
) into (
7.32
) yields that

(7.36)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)}),\bm{\nu}_{n,s+1/n}=\ell,\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s},\left\{{j,\ell}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right),\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s}(i)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{K^{3}}{(cn-K)^{2}}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{j,\ell}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right),\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s}(i)}\right).

Moreover, analogously to the proof of
Equation

7.14
,

(7.37)

\displaystyle\sum_{j,\ell\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{i,j}\right\}\in\PR(\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}),\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s}(i)}\right)\leq 2\delta(n+P)^{2}.

Combining
Equation

7.3
,
Equation

7.28
,
Item

1
,
Equation

7.36
and
Equation

7.37
gives

(7.38)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left({\mathfrak{E}_{n,1}(i)}\right)\leq 2\delta K^{3}c^{-2}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

2.

We next establish an upper bound on
\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{E}_{n,2}(i)}\right)
from (
7.29
). As in the previous case, by
Proposition

3.11
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{E}_{n,2}(i)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{K}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\sum_{j\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\big(j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}(,i)}),{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\},

(7.39)

\displaystyle\qquad\left\{{j,j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n},\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s+1/n}(i)\big)+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Using the same conditioning approach as in
eq.

7.32
and (
1
) (first conditioning on
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}
, then on
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
), we may first bound the following conditional probabilities on the support of column
i
in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}
and column
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
in
\bm{A}_{n,s}
.
Given
0\leq k\leq K
and
1\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n
,
by
Equation

7.19
,

(7.40)

\displaystyle\mathds{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}\mathbb{P}\left({{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)\leq\frac{K^{2K}}{c^{K}n^{k}}(1+\bar{o}_{n}(1)).

Furthermore, for
j\in[n]
, by
Equation

7.34
,

(7.41)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}(,i)}),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}}\right)\leq\frac{K^{2}}{cn-K}.

Combining
eq.

7.40
and
eq.

7.41
with the outlined conditioning, we obtain the upper bound

(7.42)

\displaystyle\frac{K^{2K+2}}{c^{K+1}n^{k+1}}(1+\bar{o}_{n}(1))\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{j,j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right),\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s+1/n}(i)}\right)

for the single summands in
item

2
.
Finally, analogously to the proof of
Equation

7.21
,

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<j_{2}\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({\left\{{j,j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right),\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s+1/n}(i)}\right)

(7.43)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\delta(k(n+P)^{k}+(k+1)(n+P)^{k+1}).

Combining
Equation

7.41
,
Equation

7.42
and
Item

2
gives that

(7.44)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left({\mathfrak{E}_{n,2}(i)}\right)\leq\delta(K+1)K^{2K+2}c^{-K-1}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

3.

Finally, we establish an upper bound on
\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\mathfrak{E}_{n,3}(i)}\right)
from (
7.29
).
Observe that on
\mathfrak{G}_{n,1}(i)
,
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}(,i)})
, so that, as in the previous two cases, by
eq.

7.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\mathfrak{E}_{n,3}(i)}\right)\leq\sum_{j\in[n]}\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}(,i)}),j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})}),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\right)+\bar{o}_{n}(1).

By the tower property,

(7.45)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}(,i)}),j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})}),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}{\left\{{j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}(,i)}),\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\right\}}\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)}\right].

On the other hand, by
Equation

7.17
,

(7.46)

\displaystyle\mathds{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}\mathbb{P}\left({j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)\leq\frac{K^{2}}{cn-K}\mathds{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}.

Hence, by

Equation

7.45
and
Equation

7.46
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n,1}(i)\cap\mathfrak{E}_{n,3}(i)}\right)\leq\frac{K^{2}}{cn-K}\mathbb{E}\left[{\sum_{j\in[n]}\mathds{1}{\left\{{j\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})}\right\}}}\right]+\bar{o}_{n}(1)\leq\frac{K^{2}}{cn-K}+\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Plugging in (
7.27
), (
7.28
) and the last three cases into (
7.26
)
finally gives that

(7.47)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i\in[n]}\mathbb{P}(\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)^{c})\leq 2\delta(K+1)K^{2K+2}c^{-K-1}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Proof of implication (
7.4
) by contraposition.
Observe that if supp
(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))=\emptyset
, given
\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
, clearly the premise of
section

7.4
is not satisfied and there is nothing to prove. We thus assume supp
(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))\not=\emptyset
. Suppose that
\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)

holds and that supp
(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))
is neither a proper relation of
\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
nor of
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
.
On
\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)
,

\displaystyle{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket(,i)})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket(,i)}),

so that we may regard supp
(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))
as the set of non-zero entries of the
i
th column in any of these matrices, as this is unaffected by the perturbation and replacing rows
i,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
by a zero rows and column
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
by a zero column. Moreover, again on
\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)
,

\displaystyle\bm{A}_{n,s}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]=\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket=\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)\llbracket{;i}\rrbracket,\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket[\bm{\theta}]=\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket=\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)\llbracket{;i}\rrbracket,

so that the assumption on proper relations concerns the
i
th column of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
and
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
, respectively.
Since the support may still form a (non-proper) relation, we distinguish four cases:

Case 1:

{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)})
has no representation in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
and no representation in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
.

As there is no representation of the non-zero entries of the
i
th column of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
,
this column cannot lie in the column space of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket
. Thus, by the second equivalence in
Equation

3.4
,
i
is frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
. Applying the same reasoning to
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
yields
i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)\cap\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)
.

Case 2:

supp
(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))
has a representation both in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
and in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
.

Since we also assume that supp
(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))
is not a proper relation in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
and in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
, all variables in
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)})
are frozen both in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
and in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
. In particular, column
i
of
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
is contained in the column space of
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket
and column
i
of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
is contained in the column space of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket
. Thus, by the second equivalence in
Equation

3.4
,
i
is neither frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
nor in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
.

Case 3:

supp
(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))
has a representation in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
, but none in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
.

As in
Case 2
, all variables in
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)})
must be frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
, but there is a variable that is not frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
.
However, this cannot happen on
\mathfrak{S}_{n,2}(i)
.

Case 4:

supp
(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))
has a representation in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
, but none in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}
.

By the same reasoning as in Case 3, this cannot happen on
\mathfrak{S}_{n,2}(i)
.

Excluding proper relations.
By (
7.4
),

(7.48)
