\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(i\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)\Delta\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i),\text{supp}(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))\text{ is a proper relation in }\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}\text{ or in }\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i)^{c}}\right).

Employing the same conditioning approach as in
item

1
and
item

1
once more, we obtain

(7.49)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i),\text{supp}(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))\text{ is a proper relation of }\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{k=2}^{K}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathds{1}{\left\{{\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}}\right)}\right\}}\mathds{1}{\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s}(i)}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s},\text{supp}(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s,\langle{i}\rangle}\rrbracket}}\right)\Bigg]+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Analogously to
Equation

7.19
,

(7.50)

\displaystyle\mathds{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s}\mathbb{P}\left({{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i)})=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}}\right)\leq\frac{K^{2K}}{c^{K}n^{k}}(1+\bar{o}_{n}(1)).

Moreover, by
3.9
,

(7.51)

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq j_{1}\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\mathds{1}{\left\{{\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}}\right)}\right\}}\mathds{1}{\mathfrak{S}_{n,3,s}(i)}\Bigg]\leq\delta K^{2K}c^{-K}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Using the tower property in (
7.49
) (conditioning on
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
) in combination with (
7.50
) and (
7.51
), we conclude that

(7.52)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i),\text{supp}(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}}\right)}\right)\leq\delta K^{2K+1}c^{-K}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Analogously,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i),{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}(,i)})\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}}\right)}\right)\leq\delta K^{2K+1}c^{-K}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Hence,

(7.53)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathfrak{S}_{n}(i),\text{supp}(\bm{A}_{n,s}(,i))\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}}\right)\cup\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;i}\rrbracket^{T}}\right))\leq 2\delta K^{2K+2}c^{-K}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1),

Combining (
7.48
), (
7.47
) and (
7.53
) yields the claim.∎

8
Conditional degree of the last awakened vertex in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}

Recall that the vector
\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}
represents the type distribution with respect to the perturbed adjacency matrix
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
, which is determined by
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
and the perturbation
\mathbf{\Theta}
.
In order to apply
Proposition

6.3
to derive the type fixed-point equations, the next step is to estimate
\mathbb{P}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n})
from
eq.

6.8
.

The present section prepares this estimate:
To determine the type distribution of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
given
\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}
, we first compute the current degree distribution of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
conditionally on
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
.
Let

(8.1)

\displaystyle q_{k}:=q_{k}(s)=\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{K}\ell\binom{\ell-1}{k}\left({\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{k}\left({1-\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{\ell-k-1}\mathrm{e}^{-\ell t_{s}}p_{\ell}

be the coefficient of
\alpha^{k}
in
\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}(\alpha)
as defined in
eq.

5.18
. Intuitively,
q_{k}
approximates the unconditional probability that a uniform vertex in a uniformly chosen edge in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
has degree
k
. The main result of the present section is the following:

Proposition 8.1
.

Uniformly in
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)-q_{k}}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Proposition

8.1
states that
the distribution of the current degree of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}

is approximately independent of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
.
This result reflects the fact that the macroscopic structure of the graph
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
is constrained by inequalities such as
Lemmas

5.4
and
5.5
, or informally, laws of large numbers, and therefore that
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
only provides limited additional information about
\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}
.

Our proof strategy towards
Proposition

8.1
is as follows: Recall that for
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
, with high probability,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is awakened by
Step 3
. Therefore, its current degree in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
is strictly smaller than its original degree. Therefore, by the law of total probability, on the event that
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is awakened by
Step 3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)

(8.2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{K}\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell}\right)\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right).

The benefit of the decomposition of (
8
) is as follows: While the current degree
\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}
is a priori rather complicated to work with, the original degree
d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}
is more closely related to the graph exploration, as sleeping vertices are awakened with probability proportional to their
original degrees
in
Step 1
and
Step 3
. Thus, coming from the graph exploration, it seems like a more reasonable task to compute
\mathbb{P}(d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket})
.

On the other hand, the probability that the current degree
\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}
of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
takes on a certain value given its original degree can be computed by a similar counting argument as in
Lemma

5.9
.
While we provide details on this counting argument in the proof of
Proposition

8.1
in Section
8.4
, the main work in this section goes into the analysis of the conditional distribution of the original degree of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
.
Through a careful examination of the graph exploration, we prove in
Lemma

8.2
that
\mathbb{P}(d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket})
is close to a constant:

Lemma 8.2
.

For any
\ell\in\left\{{0}\right\}\cup[K]
, uniformly in
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

(8.3)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)-\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

In the same spirit as
Proposition

8.1
,
Lemma

8.2
demonstrates that the conditional probability
\mathbb{P}(d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket})
is close to the unconditional one. We first prove
Lemma

8.2
in Section
8.3
and then
Proposition

8.1
in Section
8.4
. The next two sections serve as a preparation towards the proof of
Lemma

8.2
.

Remark 8.3
.

In terms of the graph exploration, the conditional probability in
Lemma

8.2
still is not easy to compute,
as it conditions on the future graph
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
, while seeking information about the past (the last removed vertex
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
).
One could consider applying Bayes’ rule to exchange the event and the condition. However, the probability
\mathbb{P}\left({{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}=G\mid d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell}\right)
remains challenging to compute, and we still need to prove that
d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}
and
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
are approximately independent. So ultimately, this approach would not differ significantly from our current one.
∎

8.1
The original and a modified graph exploration

Throughout this section, we fix
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
and an integer
r>0
. The proof of
Lemma

8.2
, which is the main technical part in this section, is based on a ‘law-of-large-numbers’ argument.
For a start, it might not be too far-stretched to believe that from the perspective of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
, the original degrees of the last
r
removed vertices all behave similarly in the sense that for
u\in[r]
,

(8.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)\approx\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right).

Thus, one could hope that the desired probability can be computed as an average over these last
r
removed vertices:

(8.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)\approx\frac{1}{r}\mathbb{E}\left[{\left|{\left\{{u\in[r]:d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}}=\ell}\right\}}\right|\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right].

More specifically, at least informally, as we first let
n\to\infty
and then
r\to\infty
,
\frac{1}{r}\left|{\left\{{u\in[r]:d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}}=\ell}\right\}}\right|
should converge to a constant by ‘the law of large numbers’. Hence, the right-hand side of
eq.

8.5
converges to a constant, and so does the left-hand side.
The proof of
Lemma

8.2
is essentially based on this idea.

In establishing a rigorous version of
eq.

8.5
, the definition of a slightly modified graph exploration will be instrumental. For this purpose, we first revisit the graph exploration from
[
28
]
, as explained in
Section

3.4
, and make all involved randomness completely explicit – that is, the i.i.d. exponential life-times and the uniform choices of half-edges in
Step 1
and
Step 2
. Let
\mathcal{H}
be the set of half-edges in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}
. We furthermore set

•

\left({\bm{E}_{h}}\right)_{h\in\mathcal{H}}
to be the i.i.d.
lifetimes
of the half-edges used in
Step 3
, each following an
{\rm Exp}({1})
distribution; and independently given these lifetimes,

•

(\bm{U}(h,t,v))_{h\in\mathcal{H},t\in\left\{{0}\right\}\cup\left\{{\bm{E}_{a}:a\in\mathcal{H}}\right\},v\in\left\{{\text{active},\text{dead}}\right\}}
to be i.i.d.
decision variables
associated to triples consisting of a half-edge
h
, a potential time
t
at which
Step 1
or
Step 2
can be called (initially or after performance of
Step 3
), and state
v
, each following a uniform distribution on
[0,1]
.

The existence of all these random variables on the same probability space is guaranteed by the Kolmogorov extension theorem. We imagine that the decision random variables
\bm{U}(h,t,v)
associated to half-edge
h
can be used to set
h
to state
v
at time
t
. Finally, recall that in the
original
graph exploration, as time
t\geq 0
evolves, the process successively goes through
Step 1
to
Step 3
, where
Step 1
and
Step 2
are performed instantaneously, while it takes an exponential time to complete
Step 3
. Thus, in terms of the exponential and decision random variables, the original graph exploration can be formulated as follows:

Step 1

At times
t
when there is no active half-edge (such as at time
t=0
), we instantaneously choose the half-edge associated to the
smallest decision variable
among the subset of decision variables that are indexed by half-edges that are sleeping at time
t
, time
t
and state
v=
active. We awaken its adjacent vertex and activate all its half-edges.
The process stops if there is no sleeping half-edge left.

Step 2

At times
t
when there is an active half-edge, pick the one associated to the
smallest decision variable
among all decision variables indexed by half-edges that are active at time
t
, time
t
and state
v=
dead. Change the status of this half-edge to dead.

Step 3

Wait until the next half-edge dies because of the time exceeding its lifetime. This half-edge is
joined to the one killed in
Step 2
to form an edge of the
graph. If the vertex it belongs to is sleeping, we change the status of this vertex
to awake and all its remaining adjacent half-edges to active. Repeat from
Step 1
.

We call
\bm{\eta}:=(\bm{E}_{h},\bm{U}(h,t,v))_{h\in\mathcal{H},t\in\left\{{0}\right\}\cup\left\{{\bm{E}_{a}:a\in\mathcal{H}}\right\},v\in\left\{{\text{active},\text{dead}}\right\}}
the
decision vector
that almost surely uniquely determines the original graph exploration as described above. Unless stated otherwise, in this section, all variables in the graph exploration, such as
\bm{\nu}_{s}
, are derived from the original graph exploration determined by the decision vector
\bm{\eta}
. When we explicitly want to emphasize this, we also write
\bm{\nu}_{s}(\bm{\eta})
.

We next define a modified graph exploration that is coupled to the original one. While it is rather similar to the latter, it will be easier to analyze a certain
switching
procedure in the modified exploration.
More specifically, given a decision vector
\bm{\eta}
, the
modified
graph exploration will proceed exactly as the original one, using
Step 1
,
Step 2’
and
Step 3
, where
Step 2’
is the following modification of
Step 2
:

Step 2’

If the number of awakened vertices in the graph is at least
\lfloor ns+2-r\rfloor
and at most
\lfloor ns\rfloor
at time
t
, and there are active half-edges belonging to the first
\lfloor ns+1-r\rfloor
awakened vertices, pick the active half-edge associated to the smallest decision variable among all decision variables indexed by half-edges that are active at time
t
and belong to one of
the first
\lfloor ns+1-r\rfloor
awake vertices
, time
t
and state
v=
dead. Change the status of this half-edge to dead.
Otherwise, pick the active half-edge associated to the smallest decision variable among all decision variables indexed by half-edges that are active at time
t
, time
t
and state
v=
dead and change the status of this half-edge to dead.

Step 2’
proceeds exactly as
Step 2
, except when the number of awakened vertices in the graph lies between
\lfloor ns+2-r\rfloor
and
\lfloor ns\rfloor
. In the time-window between the awakening of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n}
and
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
(including the steps where
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n}
and
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
are awakened), the aim of
Step 2’
is to actively suppress the possibility that half-edges adjacent to vertices
\bm{\nu}_{s-(r-2)/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s}
, are paired by other decision mechanisms than
Step 3
.
In particular, vertices are awakened in the same order both in the original and in the modified graph explorations, so that we can unambiguously use the notation
\bm{\nu}_{s}
for both.

On a high level, the modified graph exploration facilitates a proof of
eq.

8.4
because it is more stable with respect to small modifications in
\bm{\eta}
in the following sense: Fix
\bm{\eta}
such that
Step 1
is not performed between the awakening of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n}
and
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
in either graph exploration. For
u\in[r]
, denote by
\bm{h}_{u}:=h_{u}(\bm{\eta})
the first dead half-edge of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}
with respect to the original graph exploration, so that among the half-edges in
\left\{{\bm{h}_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
,
\bm{h}_{r}
dies first and
\bm{h}_{1}
dies last. Now given
u_{1},u_{2}\in[r]
with
u_{1}<u_{2}
, suppose that we modify
\bm{\eta}
to
\bm{\eta}^{\prime}
by keeping everything fixed, apart from exchanging the exponential life-times
E_{\bm{h}_{u_{1}}},E_{\bm{h}_{u_{2}}}
. This change has the effect that in the original graph exploration with respect to
\bm{\eta}^{\prime}
, as
Step 1
is not performed, the
\lfloor ns+2-u_{2}\rfloor
th awakened vertex is
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u_{1}/n}(\bm{\eta})
.
Consequently, in the next passes through
Step 2
, in comparison to the exploration with respect to
\bm{\eta}
, other decision variables are available (first only the ones corresponding to edges adjacent to
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u_{1}/n}(\bm{\eta})
), and if these are chosen, the seemingly innocent swap might lead to an avalanche of changes, finally resulting in a significantly altered graph.
On the other hand, in the modified graph exploration,
if
there are always enough active half-edges,
Step 2’
excludes the possibility to choose from the altered decision variables, so that effectively, the lifetime-exchange only swaps
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u_{1}/n}
and
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u_{2}/n}
along with the neighbors of
\bm{h}_{u_{1}}
and
\bm{h}_{u_{2}}
, but otherwise yields the same graph as the modified graph exploration with the original life-times.

Denote by
\bm{H}_{r}:=\bm{H}_{r}(\bm{\eta})
the set of all half-edges of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
according to the original and modified graph explorations. Observe that also the first dead half-edge of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}
,
u\in[r]
, does not depend on the specific exploration, so that we again use identical notation
\bm{h}_{u}
in both cases.

Next, we
identify an appropriate event
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}
which ensures that the original and modified graph exploration are identical:
Define
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}
(
\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
) to be the set of all decision vectors
\eta
such that, for the original (modified) graph exploration it holds true that:

1.

All vertices
\nu_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\nu_{s+1/n}
are activated by
Step 3
;

2.

At the time when the awakening step of
\nu_{s+1/n}
finishes, exactly one of its half-edges has been killed for each vertex
\nu_{s+2/n-u/n}(\mbox{for }u\in[r])
;

By definition, the lifetime of
h_{1}
, which is the first dead
half-edge of
\nu_{s+1/n}
, is the maximum of the lifetimes of all half-edges in
\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
. On the other hand, item
2
in the definition of
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}
(
\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
) implies that the lifetime of
h_{1}
is smaller than the minimum of the lifetimes of the half-edges in
H_{r}\backslash\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
.

Claim 8.1
.

On
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}
, both the original and the modified explorations perform exactly the same.
In particular,
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
.

Proof.

By definition, both explorations perform exactly the same until the
\lfloor ns+2-r\rfloor
th vertex has been awakened. For
\eta\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}
, this vertex is awakened by
Step 3
. In the time interval until vertex
\nu_{s+1/n}
is awakened,
Step 2
and
Step 2’
only differ if there are both active half-edges belonging to the first
\lfloor ns+1-r\rfloor
awakened vertices and active half-edges belonging to the already awakened vertices among
\nu_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\nu_{s}
, and in
Step 2
, one of the latter is killed. However, in that case, the adjacent vertex would have at least two killed half-edges at the time when the awakening step of
\nu_{s+1/n}
finishes, which cannot happen on
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}
. Thus, the original and modified graph explorations agree until vertex
\nu_{s+1/n}
is awakened. As they also perform the same afterwards, the claim follows.
∎

We will now show that
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}
happens with high probability. Thus, with decent probability, the original and modified graph explorations are the same, and we can transfer any exchangeability properties from the modified graph exploration back to the original one.

Claim 8.2
.

For any integer
r>0
,

(8.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r})=1+\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Proof.

By
Lemma

5.8
and

Equation

7.3
,

(8.7)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mbox{$\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ are activated by \ref{exploration_step31}},\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+2/n}}\right)=1+\bar{o}_{n}(1).

It thus remains to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\exists\ u\ \in[r]\mbox{ s.t. at least two half-edges of $\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}$ are killed when $\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is awakened}}\right)=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

For this, we make the following two observations:

1.

In any iteration of
Step 2
after the awakening of
\nu_{s+2/n-r/n}
, an active half-edge is chosen uniformly at random among all currently
active
half-edges. As there is at least one active half-edge each time
Step 2
is called, at any such moment between the awakening of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n}
and
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
, the number of active half-edges is lower bounded by
\max\left\{{1,\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+2/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+2/n-r/n}\rrbracket}}\right\}
.
As a consequence, for each iteration
i
of
Step 2
after the awakening of
\nu_{s+2n-r/n}
, by
Lemmas

5.8
and
5.10
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left({\exists\mbox{$h\in\bm{H}_{r}\backslash\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}:h$ is killed in the $i$th iteration of \ref{exploration_step21} after the awakening of $\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n}$}}\right)

(8.8)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{rK}{\max\left\{{1,\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+2/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+2/n-r/n}\rrbracket}}\right\}}}\right]=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

2.

In any iteration of
Step 3
after the awakening of
\nu_{s+2/n-r/n}
, an active half-edge is chosen uniformly at random among all currently
living
half-edges.
Analogously to the previous observation, for each iteration, by
Lemma

5.10
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left({\exists\mbox{$h\in\bm{H}_{r}\backslash\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}:h$ is killed in the $i$th iteration of \ref{exploration_step31} after the awakening of $\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n}$}}\right)

(8.9)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{rK}{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+2/n}\rrbracket}}}\right]=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Now, fix a number
N>0
and let
\bm{B}=B(\bm{\eta})
be the number of iterations of
Step 2
between the awakening of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n}
and
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
. Then summing
item

1
and
item

2
over
i\in[Nr]
, by a union bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left({\exists\ u\ \in[r],\mbox{ s.t. at least two half-edges of $\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}$ have been killed when $\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is awakened}}\right)

(8.10)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\bm{B}\geq Nr)+\bar{o}_{n}(1).

On the event that
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
are activated by
Step 3
and
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+2/n}
holds,
\bm{B}\geq Nr
implies that between the awakening of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n}
and that of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
, at least
Nr
half-edges were killed in iterations of
Step 2
. On the other hand, the number of living half-edges cannot exceed
Kn
, while on
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+2/n}
, the number of sleeping half-edges in the given time frame is always bounded below by
cn
. Consequently, each time a half-edge is killed in
Step 2
, with probability at least
c/K
it is a sleeping half-edge and a new vertex is awakened. By Chebyshev’s inequality,

(8.11)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big(\bm{B}\geq Nr,\mbox{ $\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ are activated by \ref{exploration_step3}},\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+2/n}\Big)\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({{\rm Bin}\left({Nr,c/K}\right)\leq r}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Nr/(cN-K)^{2},

where
{\rm Bin}\left({n,p}\right)
denotes a binomial random variable with
n
trials and success probability
p
.
By (
8.7
),
section

8.1
and
eq.

8.11
, taking
N\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left({\exists\ u\ \in[r],\mbox{ s.t. at least two half-edges of $\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}$ are killed when $\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is awakened}}\right)

(8.12)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{P}(\bm{B}\geq Nr)+\bar{o}_{n}(1)=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Combining (
8.7
) and (
8.1
)
gives
eq.

8.6
.
∎

8.2
Switching in the modified graph exploration

In this section, we define a switching operation on the
r
first killed half-edges of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
that works well with the modified graph exploration.
Fix a permutation
\rho
on
[r]
. Then for each decision vector
\eta
, we define a new decision vector
\varrho(\eta)
by replacing the lifetime of half-edge
h_{u}
by
\bm{E}_{h_{\rho(u)}}
, for
u\in[r]
.
Finally, as we are interested in degrees, let
(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}
be a sequence of integers in
[K]
and
\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
be the subset of all decision vectors such that the original degree of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}
is equal to
w_{u}
for all
u\in[r]
. Let
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}^{m}
be the graph obtained through the modified graph exploration procedure. The following claim then clarifies the effect of this operation on the modified graph exploration and the degrees of
\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
:

Lemma 8.4
.

For each permutation
\rho
on
[r]
and
\eta\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}
,

1.

\varrho(\eta)\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{\rho(u)})_{u\in[r]}}
;

2.

{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}^{m}(\varrho(\eta))
agrees with
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}(\eta)
, except that for
u\in[r]
, the neighbor of
h_{u}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}^{m}(\eta)
becomes the neighbor of
h_{\rho^{-1}(u)}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}^{m}(\varrho(\eta))
.

As we will show in the following proof, for
\eta\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}
, the same half-edges are killed in the modified graph exploration with respect to
\eta
and with respect to
\varrho(\eta)
, except that when
h_{u}\in H_{r}
is killed in the modified graph exploration with respect to
\eta
,
h_{\rho(u)}\in H_{r}
is killed in the modified graph exploration with respect to
\varrho(\eta)
.

Proof.

In the following, let ‘step’ refer to
Step 1
,
Step 2’
or
Step 3
in the modified graph exploration, where
Step 1
is also counted there are active half-edges remaining. We note that the random variables
\bm{E}_{h}
and
\bm{U}(h,t,v)
in the decision set
\bm{\eta}
are distinct almost surely.
Therefore, almost surely, the trajectory of the set of
lifetimes
of living half-edges with respect to the number of taken steps is in bijection with the evolution of the set of living half-edges and therefore the pairing order of the whole graph exploration. The main ingredient in the proof of
Lemma

8.4
is thus the following claim:

Claim 8.3
.

For
\eta\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}
such that all its components are distinct, at any step
\ell
, the set of lifetimes of living half-edges is the same for the modified graph explorations determined by
\eta
and
\varrho(\eta)
.

Proof of
8.3
.

We use induction on the number of steps
\ell\geq 0
that have already been taken.

For
\ell=0
, no step has been taken yet, and the set of lifetimes of living half-edges is
\left\{{E_{h}:h\in\mathcal{H}}\right\}
in both modified graph explorations.

To advance the induction, assume that the claim is true when at most
(\ell-1)
steps have been taken, so that the sets of lifetimes of living half-edges after the
(\ell-1)
st step are the same in both explorations.
If the
\ell
th step is an iteration of
Step 1
, the sets of lifetimes of living half-edges do not change from step
\ell-1
to step
\ell
, since no half-edges are killed in
Step 1
. If the
\ell
th step is an iteration of
Step 3
, then in both explorations, the half-edge with the smallest lifetime among all living half-edges is killed and removed from the sets of lifetimes of living half-edges.

We now consider the case that the
\ell
th step is an iteration of
Step 2’
and emphasize that
h_{u}
,
H_{r}
and
\nu_{t}
always refer to the modified graph exploration with respect to
\eta
.
Given the set of lifetimes of living half-edges after the
(\ell-2)
th step, which has been an iteration of
Step 3
, the following four sets are determined for both
\eta
and
\varrho(\eta)
:

•

The sets of
living
half-edges: This is due to the fact that the lifetimes stand in (known) bijection to the half-edges (even though the bijections may be different for
\eta
and
\varrho(\eta)
);

•

The sets of
dead
half-edges as the complements of the sets of living half-edges with respect to
\mathcal{H}
;

•

The sets of
sleeping
half-edges: This is due to the fact that after each iteration of
Step 3
, the adjacent half-edges of vertices with only living half-edges can be unambiguously identified as sleeping.

•

The sets of
active
half-edges as the complements of the sets of sleeping half-edges with respect to the set of living half-edges.

Given this information, we in particular know the sets of awake vertices and next distinguish the cases whether we are in an epoch between the awakening of
\nu_{s+2/n-r/n}
and
\nu_{s+1/n}
or not.

1.

Suppose that in the modified graph exploration for
\eta
, after step
\ell-2
, there are at most
\lfloor ns+1-r\rfloor
or at least
\lfloor ns+1\rfloor
awakened vertices. Then up to this stage, none or all of the half-edges in
\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
have been killed in the modified graph exploration w.r.t.
\eta
and thus all or none of their lifetimes are in the set of living half-edges after the
(\ell-2)
th step. Since the bijections between a half-edge and its lifetime agree on
\mathcal{H}\backslash\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
for both modified graph explorations, we conclude from the induction hypothesis that the sets of living half-edges are the same after the
(\ell-2)
th step, and thus that all four sets above are the same.

Then in the
(\ell-1)
st step, which is an iteration of
Step 1
, if the set of active half-edges was non-empty in the
(\ell-2)
th step, no vertex is awakened, in which case it is immediately clear that all four sets above stay the same. On the other hand, if the set of active half-edges was non-empty in the
(\ell-2)
th step, a sleeping half-edge is activated according to the value of its decision variable, all of which agree in
\eta
and
\rho(\eta)
. As
\eta\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
, the corresponding awakened vertex is not one of
\nu_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\nu_{s+1/n}
in the modified graph exploration w.r.t
\eta
, and thus the lifetimes of sleeping and active vertices change in the same way. Again, we conclude that after the
(\ell-1)
st step, all four sets above agree for
\eta
and
\rho(\eta)
.

As the sets of active half-edges agree after the
(\ell-1)
st step and the choice of the killed half-edge in step
\ell
is based on identical decision variables, the same half-edge is killed in both modified graph explorations. Moreover, as
\eta\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
, the killed half-edge is not in
\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
, so that it has the same lifetime in
\eta
and
\rho(\eta)
.
We conclude that the sets of lifetimes of living half-edges are the same after the
\ell
th step.

2.

Suppose that in the modified graph exploration for
\eta
, after step
\ell-2
, there are at least
\lfloor ns+2-r\rfloor
and at most
\lfloor ns\rfloor
awakened vertices.
As execution of
Step 1
would entail awakening of a new vertex, by
item

1
in the definition of
\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
, nothing happens in the
(\ell-1)
st step in the modified graph exploration for
\eta
. Moreover, by the induction hypothesis, after step
\ell-2
, the sets of lifetimes of living, and therefore also those of dead, half-edges agree in the modified explorations w.r.t.
\eta
and
\rho(\eta)
. From this we conclude that every half-edge not in
\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
has the same status
active
,
sleeping
or
dead
in both explorations after step
\ell-2
. Therefore, if the set of active half-edges w.r.t.
\eta
is non-empty, so is the one w.r.t.
\rho(\eta)
, and nothing happens in the
(\ell-1)
st step in the modified graph exploration for
\varrho(\eta)
either.

Moreover, as the set of active half-edges
not in
\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
in the
\ell
th step in both explorations is the same and non-empty, in step
\ell
, the same active half-edge is chosen according to the value of its decision variable and killed in both explorations.
As this half-edge is not in
\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
, the same value is removed from both sets of lifetimes of living half-edges.

As a consequence, the sets of lifetimes of living half-edges are the same after the completion of the
\ell
th step.
∎

From the proof we see that at each step, every half-edge not in
\left\{{h_{u}:u\in[r]}\right\}
has the same status
active
,
sleeping
or
dead
in both explorations. Therefore, both explorations perform the same, except when the vertices
\nu_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\nu_{s+1/n}
are awakened by
Step 3
. At these times,
h_{u}
is paired in the exploration w.r.t.
\eta
, while
h_{\rho^{-1}(u)}
is paired in the exploration w.r.t.
\rho(\eta)
. This permutes the order in which the
\lfloor ns+2-r\rfloor
th,
\ldots,\lfloor ns+1\rfloor
th vertices are awakened and therefore their degrees.
It is then very easy to see that
\varrho(\eta)\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{\rho(u)})_{u\in[r]}}
, and
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}^{m}(\varrho(\eta))
agrees with
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}^{m}(\eta)
, except that for
u\in[r]
, the neighbor of
h_{u}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}^{m}(\eta)
becomes the neighbor of
h_{\rho(u)}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n}^{m}(\varrho(\eta))
.
∎

8.3
Proof of
Lemma

8.2

Proof of
Lemma

8.2
.

In the following, denote by
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}
the induced graph on the last
n-\lfloor ns+1\rfloor
sleeping vertices in the graph that results from the modified graph exploration.
Note that for each permutation
\rho
of
[r]
,
\rho^{-1}
is a permutation of
[r]
as well. Hence,
\varrho(\eta)\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{\rho(u)})_{u\in[r]}}
only if
\eta=\varrho^{-1}(\varrho(\eta))\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}
by
Lemma

8.4
, and thus
\varrho:\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}\to\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{\rho(u)})_{u\in[r]}}
is a bijection.
Moreover,
item

2
in
Lemma

8.4
gives that
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}(\eta)={\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}(\rho(\eta))
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\eta\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}\cap\left\{{\gamma:{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}(\gamma)=G}\right\}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\varrho(\eta)\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{\rho(u)})_{u\in[r]}}\cap\left\{{\gamma:{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}(\gamma)=G}\right\}.

On the other hand, since we merely exchange the lifetimes of the half-edges in
\left\{{h_{u}}\right\}_{u\in[r]}
, the probability density function of both
\bm{\eta}
and
\varrho(\bm{\eta})
under the condition
\bm{E}_{h}=e_{h}\ (h\in\mathcal{H})
equals to
\prod_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\mathrm{e}^{-e_{h}}
.
Hence,

(8.13)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\bm{\eta})}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\varrho(\bm{\eta}))}=1.

As a consequence, for any graph
G
, by
Equation

8.13
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}},{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}(\bm{\eta})=G}\right)

\displaystyle=\int_{\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}\cap\left\{{\bm{\gamma}:{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}(\bm{\gamma})=G}\right\}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\bm{\eta})

\displaystyle=\int_{\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}\cap\left\{{\bm{\gamma}:{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}(\bm{\gamma})=G}\right\}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\varrho(\bm{\eta}))

\displaystyle=\int_{\varrho(\bm{\eta})\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{\rho(u)})_{u\in[r]}}\cap\left\{{\bm{\gamma}:{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}(\bm{\gamma})=G}\right\}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}(\varrho(\bm{\eta}))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{\rho(u)})_{u\in[r]}},{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}(\bm{\eta})=G}\right).

Hence,

(8.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{\rho(u)})_{u\in[r]}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right).

Let
(m_{k})_{k\in[K]}
be a sequence of non-negative integers such that
\sum_{k=1}^{K}m_{k}=r
. Let
\hat{\mathfrak{M}}_{s,(m_{k})_{k\in[K]}}
denote the disjoint union of all
\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}
such that the number of
u\in[r]
with
w_{u}=k
is equal to
m_{k}
for all
k\in[K]
. There are
\frac{r!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}m_{i}!}
such disjoint
\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}
that belong to each
\hat{\mathfrak{M}}_{s,(m_{k})_{k\in[K]}}
and
\mathbb{P}(\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m})
is the same for all such
(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}
by
eq.

8.14
. As a consequence,

(8.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\hat{\mathfrak{M}}_{s,(m_{k})_{k\in[K]}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right)=\frac{r!}{\prod_{i\in[K]}m_{i}!}\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right),

where on the right-hand side the number of
u\in[r]
such that
w_{u}=k
is equal to
m_{k}
for all
k\in[K]
.

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right)=

\displaystyle\sum_{(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}\in[K]^{r},w_{1}=\ell}\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right).

In the summation on the right-hand side, the number of
(w_{u})_{u\in[r]}
such that the number of
u\in[r]
with
w_{u}=k
is equal to
m_{k}
for all
k\in[K]
equals
\frac{(r-1)!}{(m_{\ell}-1)!\prod_{i\in[K]\backslash\left\{{\ell}\right\}}m_{i}!}
. Hence, by
Equation

8.15
,

(8.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(m_{k})_{k\in[K]}:\\
\sum_{k=1}^{K}m_{k}=r\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\hat{\mathfrak{M}}_{s,(m_{k})_{k\in[K]}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right)\frac{m_{\ell}}{r}.

Let
\bm{m}_{\ell}
and
\bm{m}_{\ell,m}
denote the number of vertices in
\{\nu_{s+2/n-r/n},\ldots,\nu_{s+1/n}\}
that have
original degree
\ell
in the original and modified graph explorations, respectively. Then (
8.16
) yields that

(8.17)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell,m}}{r}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}}\right\}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right].

The l.h.s. of
Equation

8.17
is already close to that of
Equation

8.3
, except that the graph is modified and we are considering
\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
. These two issues can be resolved if
\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}
w.h.p., by applying
8.1
. To prove
Equation

8.3
, we also need to express the r.h.s. of
Equation

8.17
in a suitable form. For this purpose, we formulate the following claim:

Claim 8.4
.

For any
\ell\in\left\{{0}\right\}\cup[K]
, uniformly in
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

(8.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell}}{r}-\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}}\right|=\bar{o}_{r}(1)+\bar{o}_{n}(1),

where
a_{r}=\bar{o}_{r}(1)
means that
(\left|{\bm{a}_{r}}\right|)_{r\in\mathbb{N},s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]}
is uniformly bounded and
\lim_{r\to\infty}a_{r}=0
.

The proof of Claim
8.4
is deferred to the end of this section.
Now, recall that
8.1
states that, on
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}
, both explorations perform exactly the same. Hence,

(8.19)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell}}{r}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}}\right\}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell,m}}{r}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}}\right\}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right].

Since
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
by
8.1
and
\bm{m}_{\ell},\bm{m}_{\ell,m}\leq r
,
Equation

8.19
yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell}}{r}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell,m}}{r}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}}\right\}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right]}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell}}{r}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}}\right\}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell,m}}{r}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}}\right\}}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}^{m}}\right]}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+1-\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}}\right)

(8.20)

\displaystyle\qquad=1+\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}}\right)-2\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}}\right)\leq 2-2\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}}\right)=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Further,
eq.

8.6
, along with
\mathfrak{O}_{s,r}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}_{s,r}
, gives that
\mathbb{P}(\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r})=1+\bar{o}_{n}(1)
. Hence,

(8.21)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\eta}\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)}\right|\leq\mathbb{P}(\bm{\eta}\not\in\mathfrak{M}_{s,r})=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Consequently, by
eq.

8.17
,
eq.

8.18
,
section

8.3
and
eq.

8.21
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)-\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1)+\bar{o}_{r}(1).

Since
\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)-\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}}\right|
does not depend on
r
, taking the limit
\lim_{r\to\infty}
on both sides of the above equation, we conclude that the left-hand side is equal to
\bar{o}_{n}(1)
, as desired.
∎

Hence, we are left to prove
eq.

8.18
.

Proof of
8.4
.

We prove
8.4
by using a second-moment method.
For any
b\in[s+2/n-r/n,s+1/n]
, by
Lemma

5.8
and the fact that
s\mapsto t_{s}
is continuous,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{b}}=\ell}\right)=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{b}}=\ell\mid\bm{V}_{0,\llbracket{b-1/n}\rrbracket},\ldots,\bm{V}_{K,\llbracket{b-1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\ell\bm{V}_{\ell,\llbracket{b-1/n}\rrbracket}}{\sum_{k\in[K]}k\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b-1/n}\rrbracket}}}\right]

(8.22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}+\bar{o}_{n}(1),

where in the second equation we use the fact that a sleeping vertex in the graph exploration is awakened with probability proportional to its original degree.

Furthermore, for any
b_{1},b_{2}\in[s+2/n-r/n,s+1/n]
such that
b_{1}\leq b_{2}-1/n
, by
Lemma

5.8
and the tower property,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{1}}}=d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{2}}}=\ell\mid\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket},k\in[K]}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{1}}}=\ell\mid\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket},k\in[K]}\right)\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{2}}}=\ell\mid d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{1}}}=\ell,\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket},k\in[K]}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\ell\bm{V}_{\ell,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket}}{\sum_{k\in[K]}k\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket}}\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{2}}}=\ell\mid\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{i}-1/n}\rrbracket},i\in[2],k\in[K],d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{1}}}=\ell}\right)\mid\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket},k\in[K],d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{1}}}=\ell}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\ell\bm{V}_{\ell,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket}}{\sum_{k\in[K]}k\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket}}\frac{\ell\bm{V}_{\ell,\llbracket{b_{2}-1/n}\rrbracket}}{\sum_{k\in[K]}k\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{2}-1/n}\rrbracket}}\mid\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket},k\in[K],d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{1}}}=\ell}\right].

Therefore, by
Lemma

5.8
,

(8.23)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{1}}}=d_{\bm{\nu}_{b_{2}}}=\ell}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left[{\frac{\ell\bm{V}_{\ell,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket}}{\sum_{k\in[K]}k\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{1}-1/n}\rrbracket}}\frac{\ell\bm{V}_{\ell,\llbracket{b_{2}-1/n}\rrbracket}}{\sum_{k\in[K]}k\bm{V}_{k,\llbracket{b_{2}-1/n}\rrbracket}}}\right]=(\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1})^{2}+\bar{o}_{n}(1).

On the other hand, the definition of
\bm{m}_{\ell}
yields that
\bm{m}_{\ell}=\sum_{u\in[r]}\mathds{1}{\left\{{d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}}=\ell}\right\}}
. Thus,

(8.24)

\displaystyle\left({\mathbb{E}\left|{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell}}{r}-\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}}\right|}\right)^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}\left|{\frac{\bm{m}_{\ell}}{r}-\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r^{2}}\Big(\sum_{u\in[r]}\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}}=\ell}\right)+2\sum_{u\in[r]}\sum_{v\in[u-1]}\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}}=d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-v/n}}=\ell}\right)

\displaystyle-2r\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}\sum_{u\in[r]}\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+2/n-u/n}}=\ell}\right)+\left({r\ell p_{\ell}\mathrm{e}^{-t_{s}\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}}\right)^{2}\Big).

The equality in
eq.

8.18
then follows directly from the combination of
section

8.3
,
eq.

8.23
and
eq.

8.24
.
∎

8.4
Proof of
Proposition

8.1

Recall the decomposition
section

8
. While
Lemma

8.2
yields the desired estimate for the second factor
\mathbb{P}\left({d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)
on the right hand side, we now derive the asymptotics of the first factor
\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell}\right)
.
Let
\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
be the number of half-edges of vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
that are joined to a half-edge outside of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{n^{-1}\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\lambda(t_{s})+\frac{\lambda^{2}(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1)\quad\mbox{(for a proof, see Appendix \ref{ap_eventg}).}

Next, recall
5.6
and the event
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
from
eq.

7.2
. On the event
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
, for sufficiently large
n
, vertex
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is awakened via
Step 3
.
At the time when vertex
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}

is awakened, the number of active half-edges
\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
is lower bounded by
\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
, as all half-edges of vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
that are joined to a half-edge outside of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
will connect to active half-edges. Given
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
such that
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
holds,
\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
and
d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell
, the neighbors of the
\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
half-edges are chosen uniformly at random among the active half-edges.
As
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
has
\ell-1
active half-edges when it is awakened, for
k\leq\ell-1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell,\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\ell+1}{\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-k}\binom{\ell-1}{k}}{\binom{\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}{\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}}=\frac{\binom{\ell-1}{k}\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}(\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-i)\prod_{i^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-2-k}{(\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-i^{\prime})}}{\prod_{i^{\prime\prime}=0}^{\ell-2}(\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-i^{\prime\prime})}.

Define
r_{k,\ell}:=\binom{\ell-1}{k}\left({\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{k}\left({1-\frac{\lambda(t_{s})\mathrm{e}^{2t_{s}}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)^{\ell-k-1}
. By
Lemma

5.8
and
Lemma

5.10
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}\bigg\lvert\binom{\ell-1}{k}\frac{\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}(\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-i)\prod_{i^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-2-k}{(\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{C}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-i^{\prime})}}{\prod_{i^{\prime\prime}=0}^{\ell-2}(\bm{L}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-\bm{S}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}-i^{\prime\prime})}-r_{k,\ell}\bigg\rvert}\right]=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Then the tower property yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}{\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell}\right)-r_{k,\ell}}\right|}\right]=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Thus,

(8.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell}\right)-r_{k,\ell}}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathds{1}{\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k,\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},d_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}=\ell}\right)-r_{k,\ell}}\right|}\right]+\bar{o}_{n}(1)=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Note that
q_{k}=\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{K}\ell\mathrm{e}^{-\ell t_{s}}p_{\ell}\lambda(t_{s})^{-1}r_{k,\ell}
. The desired result then follows directly from the combination of
eq.

7.4
,
section

8
,
Lemma

8.2
,
eq.

8.25
, and the tower property.

9
Fixed-point equations

Building upon the stability results from Section
7
and the conditional degree analysis from
Section

8
, we may now derive the type fixed-point equations. In this sense, the main result of the present section is the following:

Proposition 9.1

(Type fixed-point equations)
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
. Then uniformly in
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

(9.1)

\displaystyle\bm{y}_{s}=1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}}\right)-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s}}\right)+\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1);

(9.2)

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{s}=\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}}\right)-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1);

(9.3)

\displaystyle\bm{v}_{s}=\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s}}\right)-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1);

(9.4)

\displaystyle\bm{z}_{s}\geq\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Throughout the section, to treat all four equations in a unified way,
we use the following suggestive notation for
the functions on the right-hand sides of (
9.1
) to (
9.4
):

Definition 9.2

(Type functions)
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
denote the set of non-decreasing functions
g:[0,1]\to[0,1]
and
\Delta^{4}
be the four-dimensional standard simplex. We then define the following three functions
Y,U,V:\Delta^{4}\times\mathcal{G}\to[0,1]
by setting

(i)

Y\left({\zeta,g}\right)=1-g(x+y+u)-g(x+y+v)+g(x+y)
for
(\zeta,g)\in\Delta^{4}\times\mathcal{G}
;

(ii)

U\left({\zeta,g}\right)=g(x+y+u)-g(x+y)
for
(\zeta,g)\in\Delta^{4}\times\mathcal{G}
;

(iii)

V\left({\zeta,g}\right)=g(x+y+v)-g(x+y)
for
(\zeta,g)\in\Delta^{4}\times\mathcal{G}
;

(iv)

Z\left({\zeta,g}\right)=g(y)
for
(\zeta,g)\in\Delta^{4}\times\mathcal{G}
.

∎

Recall that our proof strategy towards
Proposition

9.1
is via
Proposition

6.3
. Given that we have established the hold of assumption
eq.

6.7
in
Section

7
, it remains to establish assumption
eq.

6.8
(or
eq.

6.10
) for
\bm{w}\in\left\{{\bm{y},\bm{u},\bm{v}}\right\}
(
\bm{w}=\bm{z}
):

(9.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-W\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}}\right)=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\qquad(\text{or }\geq\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\text{ respectively}).

In order to establish
eq.

9.5
, in
Section

9.1
, we relate the conditional probability that
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
assumes a certain type in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
to the types of its neighbors in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
and
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
. We finally prove
Proposition

9.1
in
Section

9.2
.

9.1
Basic and type events

We first introduce two batches of events,
basic
and
type
. The idea is that the type events are (mostly) derived from different combinations of the basic events, and directly relate to the conditional probability that
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is of a particular type in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
.

Definition 9.3

(Basic and type events)
.

For
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
, define two basic events as follows:

(9.6)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{F}}_{s}

\displaystyle=\left\{{{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\subseteq\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\},

(9.7)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}

\displaystyle=\left\{{{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\subseteq\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)}\right\}.

Based on the two basic events, define the following five type events:

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{Y}}_{s}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{F}}^{c}_{s}\cap{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{U}}_{s}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{F}}^{c}_{s}\cap{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{V}}_{s}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{F}}_{s}\cap{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{XZ}}_{s}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{F}}_{s}\cap{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}\quad\text{and }

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\circ}_{s}

\displaystyle=\{{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\subseteq\mathcal{Y}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\}.

∎

The first two intermediate results of this subsection establish that on each type event
{\mathfrak{W}}_{s}
,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
essentially is an element of
\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)
.

Lemma 9.4
.

For any
W\in\{Y,U,V\}
and
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

(9.8)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\notin\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right),{\mathfrak{W}}_{s}\right)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1),

as well as

(9.9)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{X}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cup\mathcal{Z}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right),{\mathfrak{XZ}}_{s}\right)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

The proof of
Lemma

9.4
is given in Appendix
G.4
.

Lemma 9.5
.

For any
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{Z}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right),{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ}\right)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

The proof of
Lemma

9.5
is given in Appendix
G.5
. Having identified the close connection between the type events and the type of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
, it remains to estimate their (conditional) probabilities, which we do in the next lemma.

Lemma 9.6
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
. For any
W\in\left\{{Y,U,V}\right\}
and
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

(9.10)

\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathfrak{W}}_{s}\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-W(\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}})=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

and

(9.11)

\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ}\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{y}}\right)=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Proof.

We carry out the proof of
eq.

9.10
for
W=Y
in detail; the proofs for the other choices of
W
and
eq.

9.11
proceed along the same lines.
As
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{s}={\mathfrak{F}}^{c}_{s}\cap{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s}
, by the inclusion-exclusion principle,

(9.12)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathfrak{Y}}_{s}}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathfrak{F}}^{c}_{s}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathfrak{F}}^{c}_{s}\cup{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s}}\right)=1-\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathfrak{F}}_{s}}\right)-\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left({{\mathfrak{F}}_{s}\cap{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}}\right).

Moreover, by Definitions
3.7
and
9.3
,

(a)

{\mathfrak{F}}_{s}=\{{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\subseteq\mathcal{X}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cup\mathcal{Y}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cup\mathcal{V}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\}
;

(b)

{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}=\{{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\subseteq\mathcal{X}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cup\mathcal{Y}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cup\mathcal{U}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\}
;

(c)

{\mathfrak{F}}_{s}\cap{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}=\{{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\subseteq\mathcal{X}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cup\mathcal{Y}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\}
.

Let
f_{d}:[0,1]\to[0,1]
be defined by
f_{d}(x)=x^{d}
. Instead of starting at (
9.10
) directly, we first show that

(9.13)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{Y}_{s}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta},\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}\right)-Y\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},f_{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}\right)}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1),

where as always,
\mathbf{\Theta}
is the pair of perturbation matrices.

Next, recall from
eq.

7.2
that
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
denotes the event that the number of
j\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
such that
\bar{\bm{d}}_{j,s+1/n}<d_{j}
is greater than
cn
and from
eq.

7.3
that
\mathbb{P}(\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n})=1-\bar{o}_{n}(1)
. In what follows, we will thus assume that
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
has the corresponding degree properties.
Conditionally on such
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
,
\mathbf{\Theta}
and
\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}
, and additionally on the event that
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
does not have any self-loops, by
7.1
, the neighboring half-edges of
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s}\rrbracket}
are chosen uniformly at random without replacement from all the half-edges adjacent to vertices in
\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
which have not been paired in
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}
. Denote the number of those possible half-edges by
\bm{m}_{s+1/n}=\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n})
. Out of these,
\sum_{i\in\mathcal{V}_{\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}(d_{i}-\bar{\bm{d}}_{i,s+1/n})\mathds{1}{\left\{{i\in\mathcal{Y}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right\}}=\bm{m}_{s+1/n}\bm{y}_{s+1/n}
belong to a vertex with type
Y
in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
. Therefore, by
9.2
and
Equation

9.12
,

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{Y}_{s}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta},\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}\right)-Y\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},f_{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\Bigg|\frac{\binom{(\bm{x}_{s+1/n}+\bm{y}_{s+1/n}+\bm{v}_{s+1/n})\bm{m}_{s+1/n}}{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}{\binom{\bm{m}_{s+1/n}}{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}-(\bm{x}_{s+1/n}+\bm{y}_{s+1/n}+\bm{v}_{s+1/n})^{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}+

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\frac{\binom{(\bm{x}_{s+1/n}+\bm{y}_{s+1/n}+\bm{u}_{s+1/n})\bm{m}_{s+1/n}}{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}{\binom{\bm{m}_{s+1/n}}{{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}}-(\bm{x}_{s+1/n}+\bm{y}_{s+1/n}+\bm{u}_{s+1/n})^{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\frac{\binom{(\bm{x}_{s+1/n}+\bm{y}_{s+1/n})\bm{m}_{s+1/n}}{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}{\binom{\bm{m}_{s+1/n}}{{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}}+(\bm{x}_{s+1/n}+\bm{y}_{s+1/n})^{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\mathbb{P}\left({\mbox{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ has self-loop(s)}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}\right).

We next bound the terms above separately.

1.

For each integer
0\leq k\leq N
,

\displaystyle 0\leq 1-\frac{\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}(N-i)}{N^{k}}\leq\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{i}{N}=\frac{k(k-1)}{2N}.

As a consequence, for each integer
0\leq k\leq N_{1}\leq N_{2}
,

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{\binom{N_{1}}{k}}{\binom{N_{2}}{k}}-\left({\frac{N_{1}}{N_{2}}}\right)^{k}}\right|\leq\frac{N_{1}^{k}}{\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}(N_{2}-i)}\frac{k(k-1)}{2N_{1}}.

Hence, for
a\in[0,1]
and
k\leq K
, as
\bm{m}_{s+1/n}\geq cn
on
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
,

\displaystyle\left|{\frac{\binom{a\bm{m}_{s+1/n}}{k}}{\binom{\bm{m}_{s+1/n}}{k}}-a^{k}}\right|\leq\frac{a^{k-1}}{\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}(1-i/\bm{m}_{s+1/n})}\frac{k(k-1)}{2\bm{m}_{s+1/n}}=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

2.

On
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
, there are at least
cn
living half-edges when
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is awakened. Consequently,
by
7.1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mbox{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ has at least a self-loop},\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}}\right)\leq K\frac{K}{cn}=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Consequently, restricted to
\mathfrak{G}_{\varepsilon,s+1/n}
,
Equation

9.13
holds, from which the unrestricted version follows using
Equation

7.3
.
Hence,

(9.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{Y}_{s}\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left[{Y\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},f_{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right]}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad=\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{E}\left[{\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{Y}_{s}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta},\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}\right)-Y\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},f_{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right]}\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{Y}_{s}\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta},\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}\right)-Y\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},f_{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}}}\right)}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

On the other hand, recall
q_{k}(s)
from (
8.1
), which is the coefficient of
\alpha^{m}
in
\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}(\alpha)
.
By the fact that

\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket}}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta}}\right),

Proposition

8.1
and the tower property, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-q_{k}(s)}\right|\leq\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{s+1/n}\rrbracket},\mathbf{\Theta}}\right)-q_{k}(s)}\right|=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Consequently,

(9.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\mathbb{E}\left[{Y(\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},f_{\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}})\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right]-Y(\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}})}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{\sum_{k=0}^{K}Y(\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},f_{k})\mathbb{P}\left({\bar{\bm{d}}_{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},s}=k\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-Y(\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}})}\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left|{Y\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},\sum_{k=0}^{K}q_{k}(s)f_{k}}\right)-Y(\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}})}\right|+\bar{o}_{n}(1)=\bar{o}_{n}(1).

Combining
Equation

9.14
and
Equation

9.15
gives
Equation

9.10
for
W=Y
.
∎

9.2
Proof of
Proposition

9.1

With
Lemmas

9.4
,
9.5
and
9.6
in hand, we are now able to prove
Proposition

9.1
.
It follows directly
from the combination of
Lemmas

9.4
,
9.5
and
9.6
that for any
W\in\left\{{Y,U,V}\right\}
and
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

(9.16)

\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{W}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-W\left({\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n},\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}}\right)=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

while

(9.17)

\mathbb{P}\left({\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{Z}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\mid\bm{\zeta}_{s+1/n}}\right)-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{y}_{s+1/n}}\right)\geq\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Combining (
9.16
) and (
9.17
) with
Proposition

6.3
yields the conclusion of
Proposition

9.1
.

10
Proof of
Proposition

4.2
: Lower bound on the rank

Recall that we fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
and let
\iota=1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon
. As outlined, our strategy to lower-bound the asymptotic rank is a telescoping decomposition according to the graph exploration. In particular, recall from
Section

6.2
that we build on the lower bound

(10.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{ns=\lfloor n\varepsilon\rfloor}^{\lceil n\iota\rceil-1}\left({\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)-\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]}\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,\iota}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right].

In this section, we first derive a lower bound on the individual summands
\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)-\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]
in
Section

10.1
and then prove
Proposition

4.2
in
Section

10.2
.

10.1
Rank-difference: From fixed-point equations to lower bound

Already in
eq.

6.6
, we derived the expression

(10.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)-\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{x}_{s}+2\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s}}\right]+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1)

of the expected rank decrease in terms of the different variable types. Combining (
10.2
) with the fixed-point equations
eq.

9.1
and
eq.

9.2
and the deterministic relation

(10.3)

\displaystyle\bm{\alpha}_{s}=\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s},

we obtain

(10.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)-\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[{\bm{\alpha}_{s}+1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}(\bm{\alpha}_{s})}\right]+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1)=:\mathbb{E}\left[{h_{t_{s}}(\bm{\alpha}_{s})}\right]+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

In the last step, we have defined the function
h_{t}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
given by

(10.5)

\displaystyle h_{t}\left({\alpha}\right):=\alpha+1-\hat{\psi}_{t}\left({\alpha}\right).

Unfortunately,
\bm{\alpha}_{s}
in
eq.

10.4
is a rather complicated random variable and might not even converge in an appropriate sense. Nevertheless, the type fixed-point equations in
Proposition

9.1
also open the door towards the derivation of a lower bound on
\mathbb{E}[h_{t_{s}}(\bm{\alpha}_{s})]
,
up to an
\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
error.
To formulate the lower bound, we define the continuous function
G_{t}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
given by

(10.6)

\displaystyle G_{t}(\alpha):=\alpha+\hat{\psi}_{t}(1-\hat{\psi}_{t}(\alpha))-1

and let
\alpha^{\star}(t)
be the largest zero of
G_{t}
in
[0,1]
. Then we have the following lower bound on the rank difference:

Lemma 10.1
.

Fix
\varepsilon\in(0,1/2-\sigma(-\ln\xi)/2)
.
For any
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
and
h_{t}
as in
eq.

10.5
,

(10.7)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)-\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]\geq h_{t_{s}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{s}))+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

The proofs of
Lemma

10.1
and later results in the current section heavily depend on the properties of the functions
G_{t}
and their zeroes. We therefore take a closer look at these now. Let

1.

\alpha_{0}(t)
be the unique zero of the increasing function
\Xi_{t}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R}
given by
\Xi_{t}(\alpha)=\alpha+\hat{\psi}_{t}(\alpha)-1
. It is straightforward to check that
\alpha_{0}(t)
is always a zero of
G_{t}
.

2.

\alpha_{\star}(t)
and
\alpha^{\star}(t)
be the smallest and largest (non-necessarily distinct) zeroes of
G_{t}(\alpha)
in
[0,1]
, respectively.

Finally, set

(10.8)

\displaystyle\kappa:=-\ln{((\hat{\psi}^{\prime})^{-1}(1)+\hat{\psi}((\hat{\psi}^{\prime})^{-1}(1)))}.

Then the following results hold:

Lemma 10.2

(Properties of
G_{t}
and its zeroes)
.

Assume that the probability distribution
(p_{k})_{k\geq 0}
satisfies Assumptions
2.1
,
2.3
and
4.1
. Then for
t\in[0,1]
, the function
G_{t}
defined in
eq.

10.6
has the following properties:

1.

G_{t}
has at most
3
zeroes, and
G^{\prime}_{t}
has at most
2
zeroes.

2.

If
G_{t}(\alpha)=0
, then also
G(1-\hat{\psi}_{t}(\alpha))=0
. Specifically,
\alpha_{\star}(t)=1-\hat{\psi}_{t}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)}\right)
and
\alpha^{\star}(t)=1-\hat{\psi}_{t}\left({\alpha_{\star}(t)}\right)
.

3.

G_{t}
has
1
or
3
zeroes. If
G_{t}
has
3
zeroes, then
\alpha_{\star}(t)<\alpha_{0}(t)<\alpha^{\star}(t)
.

4.

For any
\alpha>\alpha^{\star}(t)
,
G_{t}(\alpha)>0
.

5.

For
t<\kappa
,
G_{t}^{\prime}(\alpha_{0}(t))<0
,
G_{t}
has precisely
3
zeroes and both
G_{t}^{\prime}(\alpha_{\star}(t))
and
G_{t}^{\prime}(\alpha^{\star}(t))
are not equal to
0
; for
t>\kappa
,
G_{t}^{\prime}(\alpha_{0}(t))>0
and
G_{t}
has only
1
zero; for
t=\kappa
,
G_{t}^{\prime}(\alpha_{0}(t))=0
and
G_{t}
has only
1
zero. As a consequence, for
t\neq\kappa
,
G_{t}
and
G^{\prime}_{t}
have no common zero while, for
t=\kappa
, the only common zero is
\alpha_{0}(\kappa)
.

6.

The functions
t\mapsto\alpha_{\star}(t)
,
t\mapsto\alpha_{0}(t)
and
t\mapsto\alpha^{\star}(t)
are continuous on
[0,\infty)
and continuously differentiable on
[0,\infty)\backslash\left\{{\kappa}\right\}
.

7.

For a random variable
\bm{b}
, if
G_{t}(\bm{b})=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, then also

\displaystyle\min\left\{{\left|{\bm{b}-\alpha_{\star}(t)}\right|,\left|{\bm{b}-\alpha_{0}(t)}\right|,\left|{\bm{b}-\alpha^{\star}(t)}\right|}\right\}=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

8.

The function
R_{\psi_{t}}
obtains its minimum on
[0,1]
for
\alpha\in\{\alpha_{\star}(t),\alpha^{\star}(t)\}
.

The proof of
Lemma

10.2
heavily depends on
2.3
and is given in Appendix
E
. Given
Proposition

9.1
and
Lemma

10.2
, the proof of
Lemma

10.1
is then almost identical to the proof of
[
26
, Proposition 2.14]
, which is why we defer it to Appendix
G.6
.

10.2
Overview over the proof of
Proposition

4.2

An application of the lower bound of
Lemma

10.1
on the telescoping decomposition
Equation

10.2
yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]\geq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=n\varepsilon}^{n\iota-1}h_{t_{s}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{s}))+\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,\iota}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]+\bar{o}_{n,P}(n)

(10.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\int_{\varepsilon}^{\iota}h_{t_{s}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{s}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}\left[{\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,\iota}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)}\right]+\bar{o}_{n,P}(n).

Recall that
\sigma(t)=\sum_{k\geq 0}p_{k}\mathrm{e}^{-kt}
.
Equation
section

10.2
splits the proof of
Proposition

4.2
naturally into the following two lemmas:

Lemma 10.3
.

For any
S\in[0,\iota]
, with
h_{t}
as defined in (
10.5
),

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{S}h_{t_{s}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{s}))\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=\sigma\left({t_{0}}\right)R_{\psi_{t_{0}}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{0}))-\sigma\left({t_{S}}\right)R_{\psi_{t_{S}}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{S})).

Lemma 10.4
.

Let
R_{\phi}
be as defined in
eq.

2.3
. Then, for any
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
,

(10.10)

\displaystyle\lim_{n\to\infty}\sup_{J_{n}\in{\rm Sym}_{n}(\mathbb{F}^{*})}\mathbb{P}\left({\left|{\frac{1}{n}\rank_{\mathbb{F}}\left({\bm{A}_{n,\iota}}\right)-\sigma(t_{\iota})R_{\psi_{t_{\iota}}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{\iota}))}\right|\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}}\right)=0.

We next prove
Lemma

10.3
and
Lemma

10.4
in their order of appearance, and then conclude with the proof of
Proposition

4.2
.

10.2.1
Proof of
Lemma

10.3

Fix
S\in[0,\iota]
. For any
s\in[0,S]
, the definition of
t_{s}
via
eq.

5.7
ensures that
\sigma(t_{s})=1-s
and
t_{0}=0
. In particular,

\displaystyle\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}s=\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}(1-\sigma(t_{s}))=\lambda(t_{s})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t_{s}.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\sigma\left({t_{0}}\right)R_{\psi_{t_{0}}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{0}))-\sigma\left({t_{S}}\right)R_{\psi_{t_{S}}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{S}))=2-\psi(1-\hat{\psi}(\alpha^{\star}(0)))-\psi(\alpha^{\star}(0))-\psi^{\prime}(\alpha^{\star}(0))(1-\alpha^{\star}(0))

\displaystyle-\sigma\left({t_{S}}\right)\left({2-\psi_{t_{S}}(1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{S}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{S})))-\psi_{t_{S}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{S}))-\psi^{\prime}_{t_{S}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{S}))(1-\alpha^{\star}(t_{S}))}\right).

Now let
T=T(S)=t_{S}
such that
T\in[0,\sigma^{-1}(1-\iota)]
. We hence aim to prove that

(10.11)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)+1-\hat{\psi}_{t}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)}\right)}\right)\lambda(t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\qquad=2-\psi(1-\hat{\psi}(\alpha^{\star}(0)))-\psi(\alpha^{\star}(0))-\psi^{\prime}(\alpha^{\star}(0))(1-\alpha^{\star}(0))

\displaystyle\qquad\quad-\sigma\left({T}\right)\left({2-\psi_{T}(1-\hat{\psi}_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T)))-\psi_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T))-\psi^{\prime}_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T))(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))}\right).

With the abbreviations

(10.12)

\displaystyle q(T):=\int_{0}^{T}h(t)\lambda(t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)+1-\hat{\psi}_{t}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)}\right)}\right)\lambda(t)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}t

and

(10.13)

\displaystyle r(T):=\sigma\left({T}\right)\left({2-\psi_{T}(1-\hat{\psi}_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T)))-\psi_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T))-\psi^{\prime}_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T))(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))}\right).

this reduces to showing that
q(T)=r(0)-r(T)
.

As
T\downarrow 0
, both sides of
eq.

10.11
tend to
0
. Hence, it is sufficient to prove that
-q^{\prime}(T)=r^{\prime}(T)
. Looking at the left-hand side, it is immediate that

(10.14)

\displaystyle-q^{\prime}(T)=-\left({\alpha^{\star}(T)+1-\hat{\psi}_{T}\left({\alpha^{\star}(T)}\right)}\right)\lambda(T).

For the right-hand side, let
\hat{\psi}(t,\alpha):=\hat{\psi}_{t}(\alpha)
,
f(t,\alpha):=\sigma(t)\psi_{t}(\alpha)
and
g(t,\alpha):=\lambda(t)\hat{\psi}_{t}(\alpha)
. Then

(10.15)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f}{\partial t}(t,\alpha)=-\left({1+(1-\alpha)\frac{(\lambda^{\prime}(t)+\lambda(t))\mathrm{e}^{2t}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)g(t,\alpha),

(10.16)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f}{\partial\alpha}(t,\alpha)=\frac{\lambda(t)\mathrm{e}^{2t}}{\lambda(0)}g(t,\alpha),

and

(10.17)

\displaystyle r(T)=2\sigma\left({T}\right)-f(T,1-\hat{\psi}_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T)))-f(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))-\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))(1-\alpha^{\star}(T)).

Hence,

(10.18)

\displaystyle r^{\prime}(T)=

\displaystyle-2\lambda(T)+\left({1+\hat{\psi}_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T))\frac{(\lambda^{\prime}(T)+\lambda(T))\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)g(T,1-\hat{\psi}_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T)))

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}g(T,1-\hat{\psi}_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T)))\left({\frac{\partial\hat{\psi}}{\partial t}(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))+\frac{\partial\hat{\psi}}{\partial\alpha}(T,\alpha^{\star}(T)){\alpha^{\star}}^{\prime}(T)}\right)

\displaystyle+\left({1+(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))\frac{(\lambda^{\prime}(T)+\lambda(T))\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))-\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T)){\alpha^{\star}}^{\prime}(T)

\displaystyle-\frac{(\lambda^{\prime}(T)+2\lambda(T))\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))-\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}\frac{\partial g}{\partial t}(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\alpha}(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))(1-\alpha^{\star}(T)){\alpha^{\star}}^{\prime}(T)+\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T)){\alpha^{\star}}^{\prime}(T).

Since
g(t,\alpha)=\lambda(t)\hat{\psi}_{t}(\alpha)
and
\hat{\psi}_{t}(1-\hat{\psi}_{t}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})))=1-\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})
, we have

(10.19)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\hat{\psi}}{\partial t}(t,\alpha)=\frac{\partial(g/\lambda)}{\partial t}(t,\alpha)=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}\frac{\partial g}{\partial t}(t,\alpha)-\frac{\lambda^{\prime}(t)}{\lambda^{2}(t)}g(t,\alpha)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{\partial\hat{\psi}}{\partial\alpha}(t,\alpha)=\frac{1}{\lambda(t)}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\alpha}(t,\alpha),

while
g(T,1-\hat{\psi}_{T}(\alpha^{\star}(T)))=\lambda(T)(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))
. Hence,

(10.20)

\displaystyle r^{\prime}(T)=

\displaystyle-2\lambda(T)+\left({\lambda(T)+g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))\frac{(\lambda^{\prime}(T)+\lambda(T))\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))\left({\frac{\partial g}{\partial t}(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))-\frac{\lambda^{\prime}(T)}{\lambda(T)}g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))+\frac{\partial g}{\partial\alpha}(T,\alpha^{\star}(T)){\alpha^{\star}}^{\prime}(T)}\right)

\displaystyle+\left({1+(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))\frac{(\lambda^{\prime}(T)+\lambda(T))\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}}\right)g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))-\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T)){\alpha^{\star}}^{\prime}(T)

\displaystyle-\frac{(\lambda^{\prime}(T)+2\lambda(T))\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))-\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}\frac{\partial g}{\partial t}(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))(1-\alpha^{\star}(T))

\displaystyle-\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}\frac{\partial g}{\partial\alpha}(T,\alpha^{\star}(T))(1-\alpha^{\star}(T)){\alpha^{\star}}^{\prime}(T)+\frac{\lambda(T)\mathrm{e}^{2T}}{\lambda(0)}g(T,\alpha^{\star}(T)){\alpha^{\star}}^{\prime}(T)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left({\alpha^{\star}(T)+1}\right)\lambda(T)+g\left({T,\alpha^{\star}(T)}\right)=-q^{\prime}(T),

as desired.

10.2.2
Proof of
Lemma

10.4
subject to

Observe that
\bm{A}_{n,\iota}
can naturally be regarded as the adjacency matrix of the graph union of
{\mbox{\rm CM}}_{n,\llbracket{\iota}\rrbracket}
and the empty graph on vertices
\{\bm{\nu}_{1/n},\ldots,\bm{\nu}_{\iota}\}
