.

Case 6
:
Vector of type proportions.

This directly follows from
Cases 1-5
.
∎

G.4
Proof of
Lemma

9.4

We first prove an intermediate result, which establishes the desired overall equivalence of the first and last statements of
eq.

3.4
for
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
:

Lemma G.3
.

For any
\delta>0
and
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

(G.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}\right)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1),

and

(G.16)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is not firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s}\right)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Proof.

(i)

We first show (
G.15
).
By definition,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
if and only if it is frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n};}\rrbracket
. On the good event
\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
from (
6.4
), zeroing out row
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
leaves us with the matrix
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
except the
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
th column being
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})
. By
Equation

3.4
,

\displaystyle\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\text{${\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})\not\subseteq\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)$.}

On the other hand, on
{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}
,
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})\subseteq\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)
,
so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s},\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}\right)+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

(ii)

We next prove (
G.16
).
By definition, if
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is not firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
, it is not frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n};}\rrbracket
.
On the good event
\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
, zeroing out row
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
leaves us with the matrix
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
except the
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
th column being
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})
. Since
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is not frozen in this matrix, all diagonal entries are
0
and
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]=\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}}\rrbracket
on
\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
, by
Equation

3.4
,

\displaystyle\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is not firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\

\displaystyle e_{n+\bm{\theta}_{c}}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})\mbox{ is not in the row space of $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n};}\rrbracket$},\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\

\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})
is in the column space of
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
.

On the other hand, on
{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s}
,
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})\not\subseteq\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)
. This implies that both
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})
and
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})\backslash\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)
are non-empty. If additionally,
\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})
is in the column space of
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
, then
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})\backslash\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)
is a relation of
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
. Hence, by
3.9
(iii),

\displaystyle\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is not firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s},\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\text{${\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})$ is a proper relation of $\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}$.}

By
Equation

7.19
and the same argument deriving
Equation

7.52
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)\right)\leq\delta K^{2K+1}c^{-K}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is not firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s},\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left({\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})\in\PR\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}}\right)\right)\leq\delta K^{2K+1}c^{-K}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

Consequently,

\displaystyle\limsup_{P\to\infty}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sup_{J_{n}\in{\rm Sym}_{n}(\mathbb{F}^{*}),s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]}\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is not firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\delta K^{2K+1}c^{-K}.

Since the left-hand side is independent of
\delta
, letting
\delta\downarrow 0
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ is not firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s}\right)=o_{n,P}(1).

∎

Using the definition of the variable types in terms of frozen variables, we can now easily derive
Lemma

9.4
from
Lemma

G.3
:

Proof of
Lemma

9.4
.

We show the claim for each of the possible variable types separately.

Two-sided firmly frozen variables - (
9.8
) for
W=Y
:
By definition, if
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{Y}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)
, then it is not firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
or not firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
. Since
Lemma

G.3
also applies to
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
, a union bound gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{Y}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right),{\mathfrak{Y}}_{s}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ not firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}^{c}_{s}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ not firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}$},{\mathfrak{F}}^{c}_{s}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

One-sided firmly frozen variables - (
9.8
) for
W\in\{U,V\}
:
If
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{U}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)
, then, by definition, either
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is not firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
, or, if this is not the case, it is frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]

and
firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
. In the latter case, the symmetry of frailly frozen variables under transposition (see
Lemma

G.2
) implies that
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is also firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
. We conclude that if
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{U}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)
, then either
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is not firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
or
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
. Again, by a union bound and
Lemma

G.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{U}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right),{\mathfrak{U}}_{s}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ not firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}$},{\mathfrak{F}}^{c}_{s}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

The claim for
W=V
follows analogously.

Frailly frozen or two-sided not frozen variables - (
9.9
)
:
If
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{X}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cup\mathcal{Z}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)
, then by definition,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
or
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
. By a union bound and
Lemma

G.3
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{X}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cup\mathcal{Z}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right),{\mathfrak{XZ}}_{s}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]$},{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\text{$\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}$ firmly frozen in $\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}$},{\mathfrak{F}}_{s}\right)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

∎

G.5
Proof of
Lemma

9.5

The first (and main) step is to prove that on the intersection of
\mathfrak{Z}^{\circ}_{s}
with a sufficiently likely event,
the
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
th row in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
can be linearly combined by the other rows of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
, from which it follows through
Lemma

G.2
(i) that
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is not frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
.

On
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ}\cap\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
,
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
and all variables in
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
are firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
. Ideally, to derive the desired linear combination of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)
by the other rows of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
, we would like to take one representation for each
i\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
, and then simply take the
\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},i)
-weighted sum over these representations. Alas, the
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
th column of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
might contain nonzero entries, and it is not clear that for the existing representations, also the entries of column
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
sum to zero. Therefore, we are looking for representations of
i\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
that expressly do not use one of the rows in
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(,\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n})})={\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
, if such representations exist.

In fact, on
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ}\cap\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
, since any
i\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
is firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]
, it is frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket
, so there exists a representation of
i
that does not use row
i
. To take care of the other rows corresponding to elements of
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
, we define the event

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}=

\displaystyle\left\{{\text{$\forall i\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})$: $i\notin\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)});}\rrbracket}\right)\Delta\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{i;}\rrbracket}\right)$}}\right\}.

The event
\mathfrak{C}
is sufficiently likely for our purposes, as the combination of
eq.

7.19
and
Lemma

G.2
gives that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\mathfrak{C}^{c}}\right)\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{1\leq j_{1}<\ldots<j_{k}\leq n}\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\mathbb{P}\left({{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip-2.84544pt\cdot\mathbb{P}(j_{\ell}\in\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\};}\rrbracket}\right)\Delta\mathcal{F}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{j_{\ell};}\rrbracket}\right)\mid{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})=\left\{{j_{1},\ldots,j_{k}}\right\})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K^{2K+3}c^{-K}P^{-1}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

By design, on the event

\mathfrak{C}\cap{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ}
, any
i\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
is frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)});}\rrbracket
. In particular, there exists a representation
b
of
\{i\}
in
\bm{A}_{n,s+1/n}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)});}\rrbracket
with
b_{k}=0
for all
k\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
. On the good event
\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
,

(G.17)

\displaystyle b\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n};}\rrbracket=e_{n+\bm{\theta}_{c}}(i).

Thus, on the event
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ}\cap\mathfrak{C}\cap\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
, any
i\in{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})
is frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]\llbracket{\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n};}\rrbracket
.
We conclude that the
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
th row in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
can be linearly combined by the other rows of
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
(this is also true if
{\rm supp}({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}](\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n},)})=\emptyset
). Therefore, by
Lemma

G.2
,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
is not frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]^{T}
, which only leaves the possibility
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{V}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)\cup\mathcal{Z}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)
on
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ}\cap\mathfrak{C}\cap\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
.

On the other hand, since
\mathfrak{Z}^{\circ}_{s}\subseteq{\mathfrak{F}}{\left({\rm tr}\right)}_{s}
, by
Equation

3.4
,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}
cannot be firmly frozen in
\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]
on the event
{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ}\cap\mathfrak{C}\cap\mathfrak{P}_{n,s}
.
Therefore,
\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\in\mathcal{Z}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right)
and we arrive at

\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{Z}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right),{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ},\mathfrak{C}\right)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1),

i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bm{\nu}_{s+1/n}\not\in\mathcal{Z}\left({\bm{A}_{n,s}[\bm{\theta}]}\right),{\mathfrak{Z}}_{s}^{\circ}\right)\leq K^{2K+3}c^{-K}P^{-1}+\bar{o}_{n,P}(1)=\bar{o}_{n,P}(1).

∎

G.6
Proof of
Lemma

10.1

Recall
h_{t}:[0,1]\to\mathbb{R},h_{t}\left({\alpha}\right)=\alpha+1-\hat{\psi}_{t}\left({\alpha}\right)
and that
\alpha_{\star}(t)\leq\alpha_{0}(t)\leq\alpha^{\star}(t)
denote the zeros of
\alpha\mapsto G_{t}(\alpha)
.

Lemma G.4
.

For any
t\geq 0
,

(G.18)

\displaystyle h_{t}\left({\alpha_{\star}(t)}\right)=h_{t}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)}\right)\leq h_{t}\left({\alpha_{0}(t)}\right).

Proof.

By
items

2
and
3
in
Lemma

10.2
,
\alpha_{\star}(t)\leq\alpha_{0}(t)\leq\alpha^{\star}(t)
and
h_{t}\left({\alpha_{\star}(t)}\right)=h_{t}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)}\right)
. The last inequality in
eq.

G.18
follows from the concavity of
h_{t}
.
∎

Lemma G.5
.

The following inequality holds for
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
:

(G.19)

\displaystyle h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}}\right)=

\displaystyle h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Proof.

The first and second equalities in
eq.

G.19
follow directly from
eq.

9.2
and
eq.

9.3
.

Note that
\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{z}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s}=1
. Then summing up
eq.

9.1
,
eq.

9.2
, and
eq.

9.3
gives that

(G.20)

\displaystyle\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{z}_{s}=1-(\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s})=\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Then the combination of
eq.

G.20
and
eq.

9.4
gives

\displaystyle h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)=

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)=\bm{y}_{s}+1-\bm{z}_{s}+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\bm{y}_{s}+1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

and thus the last inequality in
eq.

G.19
follows.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

10.1
.

We aim to show that

(G.21)

\displaystyle h_{t_{s}}(\bm{\alpha}_{s})\geq h_{t_{s}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{s}))+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Then
Lemma

10.1
follows directly from the combination of
Equation

G.21
,
eq.

9.1
,
eq.

9.2
and
Equation

6.6
.

Define

(G.22)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}=\mathds{1}{\left\{{h_{t_{s}}^{\prime}(\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s})\geq 0}\right\}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}=1-\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}=\mathds{1}{\left\{{h_{t_{s}}^{\prime}(\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s})<0}\right\}}.

Since
\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}+\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}=1
, we divide equation
eq.

G.21
into two parts as follows:

(G.23)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\left({h_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)}\right)-h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{\alpha}_{s}}\right)}\right)\leq\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(G.24)

and

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\left({h_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)}\right)-h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{\alpha}_{s}}\right)}\right)\leq\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

In the absence of the error terms
\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and under the assumption that
\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\equiv 1
(or
\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\equiv 1
), the proof of
Lemma

10.1
would amount to an analytic treatment of the properties of
h_{t_{s}}
. Unfortunately, we have to deal with the error terms and both cases. In the ensuing argument, we therefore fall back upon Taylor’s Theorem with Lagrange Remainder,
Lemma

10.2
and the following two facts:

(i)

For any function
g
and
\upsilon\in\left\{{0,1}\right\}
, if
\upsilon a=\upsilon b
, then
\upsilon g(a)=\upsilon g(b)
;

(ii)

For a family of differentiable functions
\left({g_{s}}\right)_{s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]}
, if there exists a uniform bound
b
such that
\sup_{s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon],r\in[0,1]}\left|{g^{\prime}_{s}(r)}\right|\leq b
, then
g_{s}(\bm{a}_{s}^{\prime})=g_{s}(\bm{a}_{s})+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
for any random variables
\bm{a}_{s},\bm{a}^{\prime}_{s}\in[0,1]
,
\bm{a}_{s}^{\prime}=\bm{a}_{s}+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\in[0,1]
and
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
, since
\left|{g_{s}(\bm{a}_{s}^{\prime})-g_{s}(\bm{a}_{s})}\right|\leq b\left|{\bm{a}_{s}^{\prime}-\bm{a}_{s}}\right|
.

1.

Proof of
Equation

G.23
.

By definition of
\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}
,
\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}h_{t_{s}}^{\prime}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)\geq 0
.
For a fixed
\varepsilon>0
, Let
b=\inf_{s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]}\hat{\psi}^{\prime\prime}_{t_{s}}(0)>0
, such that
\sup_{\alpha\in[0,1]}h_{t_{s}}^{\prime\prime}\left({\alpha}\right)\leq-b
for
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
. Then by Taylor’s Theorem with Lagrange remainder, for
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}h_{t_{s}}(\bm{y}_{s})\leq

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\left({h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)-h_{t_{s}}^{\prime}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)\bm{x}_{s}-\frac{b}{2}\bm{x}_{s}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)-\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\frac{b}{2}\bm{x}_{s}^{2}.

On the other hand,
eq.

G.19
shows that for all
s\in[\varepsilon,1-\sigma(-\ln\xi)-\varepsilon]
,

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}h_{t_{s}}(\bm{y}_{s})\geq\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Hence

(G.25)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\bm{x}_{s}=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Recall from
Equation

10.3
that
\bm{\alpha}_{s}=\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s}
. We define
\bm{\alpha}^{T}_{s}=\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}
. Then
eq.

G.25
in combination with
eq.

9.1
and
eq.

9.3
implies that

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\bm{\alpha}_{s}=

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\left({\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{v}_{s}+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)}\right)=\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\left({1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hskip 2.84544pt\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}(1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}(\bm{\alpha}^{T}_{s})+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)).

Analogously,
eq.

G.25
in combination with
eq.

9.1
and
eq.

9.2
implies that
\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\bm{\alpha}^{T}_{s}=\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\left({1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{\alpha}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)}\right)
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\bm{\alpha}_{s}=\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}(1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}(\bm{\alpha}^{T}_{s})+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1))=\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}(1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{\alpha}_{s}}\right)}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)),

i.e.,

(G.26)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}G_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{\alpha}_{s}}\right)=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Let
\bm{\beta}_{s}=\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}\bm{\alpha}_{s}+\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\alpha^{\star}(t)
. Since
G_{t}(\alpha^{\star}(t))=0
,
eq.

G.26
implies that

(G.27)

\displaystyle G_{t_{s}}(\bm{\beta}_{s})=\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}G_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{\alpha}_{s}}\right)+\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}G_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t)}\right)=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Hence,
item

7
in
Lemma

10.2
implies that

\min\left\{{\left|{\bm{\beta}_{s}-\alpha_{\star}(t_{s})}\right|,\left|{\bm{\beta}_{s}-{\alpha_{0}(t_{s})}}\right|,\left|{\bm{\beta}_{s}-\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})}\right|}\right\}=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

By
Lemma

G.4
,
h_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha_{\star}(t_{s})}\right)=h_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})}\right)\leq h_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha_{0}(t_{s})}\right)
, so

h_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})}\right)\leq h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{\beta}_{s}}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\bar{\bm{\tau}}_{s}h_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{\alpha}_{s}}\right)+\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}h_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})}\right)+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

and
eq.

G.23
follows immediately.
∎

2.

Proof of
Equation

G.24
.

By definition of
\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}
,
\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}h_{t_{s}}^{\prime}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)\leq 0
. Another application of Taylor’s Theorem with Lagrange remainder to
eq.

9.2
and
eq.

9.3
as in the argument leading to
eq.

G.25

yields that
\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\bm{u}_{s}=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\bm{v}_{s}=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Hence, by
eq.

9.1
,

(G.28)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\bm{y}_{s}=\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}(1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}(\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s})+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1))\ \mbox{and }\ \bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\bm{\alpha}_{s}=\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}(\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)).

Let

\bm{\beta}_{s}^{\prime}=\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\alpha}_{s}>{\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})}}\right\}}\bm{\alpha}_{s}+\left({1-\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\alpha}_{s}>{\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})}}\right\}}}\right)\alpha^{\star}(t_{s}).

Then
\bm{\beta}_{s}^{\prime}\geq\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})
.
Since
G_{t_{s}}(\alpha^{\star}(t_{s}))=0
, by
eq.

G.28
,
eq.

9.4
and
eq.

G.20
,

\displaystyle G_{t_{s}}(\bm{\beta}_{s}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\alpha}_{s}>{\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})}}\right\}}\left({\bm{\alpha}_{s}+\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({1-\hat{\psi}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{\alpha}_{s}}\right)}\right)-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\mathds{1}{\left\{{\bm{\alpha}_{s}>{\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})}}\right\}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}+\bm{z}_{s}-1+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)}\right)\leq\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

On the other hand, by
item

4
in
Lemma

10.2
,
G_{t_{s}}(\bm{\beta}_{s}^{\prime})\geq G_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})}\right)=0
. Hence,
G_{t_{s}}(\bm{\beta}_{s}^{\prime})=\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Then the combination of
item

7
in
Lemma

10.2
and
\bm{\beta}^{\prime}_{s}\geq\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})
yields that
\bm{\beta}^{\prime}_{s}=\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, which leads to

(G.29)

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\bm{\alpha}_{s}\leq\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\bm{\beta}^{\prime}_{s}=\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}\left({\alpha^{\star}(t_{s})+\bar{o}_{\mathbb{P}}(1)}\right).

By the concavity of
h_{t_{s}}
and
\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}h^{\prime}_{t_{s}}\left({\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s}}\right)\leq 0
,
\alpha\mapsto\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{s}h_{t_{s}}\left({\alpha}\right)
is non-increasing on
[\bm{x}_{s}+\bm{y}_{s},1]
. Hence, by

eq.

G.29
,
